# docs/LOG.md — Append-only build log

Rule (master prompt §1.1): one entry per task, ≤ 5 lines. Each entry records what was
built, what test proved it, bugs found, how fixed, and numbers measured (with command).
Append only — never rewrite or delete earlier entries. Never write a number you did not
measure in-session; if unverified, write UNVERIFIED.

---

## 2026-11-18 — W1 Wave 1 close-out (TASK W1)

- W1.1 cache wiring DONE: ScopedKey + ShouldStore in degrade lookup/store/staleFor + relay; RecordHit on own-cache hits; POST /admin/cache/flush (admin scope, Tier 2) registered in guard table + server; per-tenant flush + provenance-gated semantic isolation.
- W1.2 StreamRedactor DONE: wired into handleChatStream (onChunk + emitTail before [DONE]/error frame) and handleMessagesStream; split-PEM redaction tests pass. Fixed a real regression found by TestServerStreamLifetimeSSECap: a trailing "-" held by the redactor was lost on error paths (emitTail now bypasses WriteChunk, which would re-withhold it).
- W1.3 M1.11 DONE: default global strategy "balanced"→"priority" (router.effectiveStrategy, server strategies feed, strategytree allowlist/canonical/LeafPath); "priority"+"code" now valid tree names; unset config routes in declared order.
- W1.4 bootstrap key now carries admin scope (main.go bootstrapIssuer + runInit — the operator's first key; without it a fresh install's dashboard cannot call admin routes); W1.5 keys feed surfaces "disabled". W1.6 check: no established Authorization pattern in dashboard JS — M10 leftover, web/index.html untouched.
- Migration note (A7, verbatim):
  ```
  // MIGRATION (M1.11, 2026-11-18): the default global strategy
  // changed from "balanced" (re-rank every request by latency,
  // success, cost, quality and quota) to "priority" (try targets
  // in the operator's declared chain order, no re-ranking).
  // Configs that set global_strategy explicitly are unaffected;
  // configs that relied on the unset default now route in declared
  // order. To keep the old behaviour, set
  // routing.global_strategy to "balanced" explicitly. "balanced"
  // remains a valid, selectable strategy, and "code" (cost per
  // solved task) is now accepted by config validation as declared.
  ```

## 2026-11-18 — M5 gate (budgets, quotas, pools: M5.1–M5.14)

- Gate: `./scripts/ci.sh` → CI: PASS (log /tmp/m5-ci.log); count run (`go test ./... -race -count=1 -v`, log /tmp/m5-gate-verbose.log): 2,523 top-level PASS / 0 FAIL (3,320 incl. 797 subtests), 2 SKIP, 42/42 ok packages + 2 no-test-files (M4 was 2,389 / 3,041, 41 packages; +134 top-level reconciles exactly: 124 new test funcs in new M5 test files + 10 added in modified test files; +279 total incl. 145 new subtests; RUN 3,322 = PASS 3,320 + SKIP 2; the 15 `FAIL` lines are the known release-verification stdout pattern, zero `--- FAIL`, zero failed packages).
- M5 exit gate MET: race tests prove budgets cannot be exceeded by parallel requests — per-key (TestReserveSpendParallel, TestReserveSpendParallelChat), cumulative (TestReserveSpendParallelCumulativeCap, TestCumulativeBudgetRaceParallelChat), TPM/RPM (TestKeyTrafficLimiterParallelRace), request budget incl. hedged pairs (TestRequestBudgetStrictHedgeCountsFirstAttempt + control + streaming). Audit found and fixed two real races: the cumulative cap had no reservation (ReserveSpend no-op without a key-budget row), and the strict hedge gate checked the second attempt against spentUSD alone (both loops now gate on spentUSD + first-attempt estimate; toggle-verified — the hedge test fails with the fix reverted).

## M8.14 review — PASS-WITH-NITS (2026-11-19 ~00:17 IST)

Independent review of the combos/chains CRUD slice (builder 19/19 tests). Reviewer re-ran
19/19 PASS via overlay (gemini_native_test.go stubbed — at review time the live-tree
compile blocker was gemini_native_test.go redeclared geminiTestRegistry, NOT
files_api_test.go/webhooks_api_test.go, which compile fine now; builder caveat stale
on that point). Toggle proof reproduces. 10 extra reviewer probes all reported.
Findings (full text in the review handoff; coordinator to resolve at merge):
1. REGISTRATION HAZARDS — blocking for registration, not slice code: (a) zero-group
   startup trap: main.go:2812-2814 installs engCfg.GroupChain only if
   len(routing.Groups) > 0, so combos created on a zero-group install answer 400
   "unknown routing group" until restart — fix: install GroupChain unconditionally.
   (b) Concurrent-map race: mirror writes guarded only by ComboStore.mu, which no
   reader holds (groups.go:108, legacy_completions.go:589, server.go:4995,
   config.go:2279-2282 Clone during save all range the live maps) — combo mutation
   racing in-flight traffic is a fatal concurrent map read/write. Fix: serialization
   story (server-wide RWMutex shared by mirrors + readers, or copy-on-write) + -race
   soak with concurrent combo mutations + group-addressed traffic. Pre-existing
   dashboard strategies path does NOT have this shape (SetChainStrategy + persist).
2. Header claim correction (nit): strategy mirror is feed/config-visible only, NOT
   engine-effective — resolveGroupChain rewrites group:<name>/<model> before
   effectiveStrategy, which looks up ChainStrategy(concrete-model); group chains are
   explicitOrder so no strategy hook re-ranks. Combo with strategy cheapest routes in
   declared order; fusion/pipeline/jev can never trigger via group: addressing.
   Fix: reword header (group mirror engine-visible subject to 1a; strategy mirror
   feed + cfg.Save only).
3. comboNoAuthProvider misses catalog-category-local providers (nit): implements
   ollama-wire OR loopback, never consults CatalogID — a CategoryLocal/CategoryNoAuth
   provider on a non-loopback URL is marked unusable. Fix: also resolve CatalogID, or
   correct the comment to state the implemented rule.
4. Malformed input silently accepted (nit): "mock-upstream@" → 201 (empty effort
   dropped); "mock-upstream/" → 201 (empty model dropped); trailing JSON garbage
   after first object → 201. Fix: reject empty effort/model; decode second value,
   reject if not EOF.
5. PATCH rename check runs before existence check (nit): PATCH /v1/combos/nope
   {"name":"other"} → 400 not 404. Fix: look up record (404) before validating name.
6. Scopes — reviewer disagrees in part: GETs → models (agreed, precedent
   guard.go:171); POST/PATCH/DELETE → admin, NOT chat (sibling slices propose admin
   for operator-config mutations; chat-scoped key rewriting routing groups is a
   privilege escalation vs the dashboard strategies feed, which requires admin).
   Coordinator to decide at registration.
7. PARITY — reviewer disagrees with both verdict-moving claims: §14b "Combos
   (10 files)" PARTIAL → "toward FULL" overstated (OmniRoute surface includes
   duplicate/import/export/test/enable, PARITY:703 — slice adds CRUD only, no
   dashboard UI). Correct: stays PARTIAL, stronger. §14b "Fallback chains API"
   (PARITY:1086) MISSING is NOT moved (declarative per-model fallback-chains API
   /api/fallback/chains — named-chain CRUD is a different surface). Stays MISSING.
8. Seeded-group DELETE destroys operator config (nit): NewComboStore seeds from
   config groups, so DELETE /v1/combos/<legacy-group> deletes the operator's
   config-file group + chain_strategies entry. Document in registration docs /
   handler comment.

## M8.7 review — PASS-WITH-NITS (2026-11-19 ~00:20 IST)

Independent review of the Files API slice (builder 7/7 + 17/17). Reviewer re-ran
7/7 internal/files + 17/17 TestFiles against the live worktree, gofmt clean,
go vet clean on both packages. NO overlay needed — the claimed sibling blocker
(gemini_native_test.go redeclared geminiTestRegistry) is gone (single declaration
now); the builder's three failed exec calls match the sibling-churn failure in the
M8.14 LOG entry, not this slice. Toggle proof re-run PASS. Tree untouched.
Claims verified: purpose list verified against the LIVE OpenAI reference (upload
purposes exactly assistants, batch, fine-tune, vision, user_data, evals;
assistants_output/batch_output/fine-tune-results are FileObject purposes only, not
accepted on upload); store safety verified (index temp in same dir so rename is
atomic; fsync content file, index file, directory; content committed before index;
failed index write removes content); traversal verified on every path (contentPath
only reached with ValidID-checked id; filenames metadata only, never path
components; Content-Disposition uses strconv.Quote on the sanitised name);
contract shapes verified (upload 200 OpenAI file object; list data:[] never null,
newest-first; second DELETE 404; content application/octet-stream + matching
Content-Length; no-config 503 defensive only); 400 for non-multipart JSON is from
the handler (ParseMultipartForm first), not the harness; fixtures all mock-labelled;
routes NOT registered; FilesRoutes() exactly five rows, all chat; M8.8 readiness
(Create/Get/List/Delete/Open stable and sufficient for batch .jsonl input; no
list-by-purpose filter, M8.8 filters client-side); list ordering deterministic
(created_at unix seconds, ties break by id desc).
Findings:
1. Store mutex held across the entire streaming upload (nit): Create takes mu
   before CreateTemp and holds it through io.Copy/Sync/rename/fsync/index save —
   a large/slow upload serialises every other store op. Fix before/with M8.8:
   stream to the temp file BEFORE taking the lock; hold it only for rename +
   index load/save.
2. Dir-fsync failure after a successful index rename leaves a dangling index entry
   (nit): saveIndexLocked renames first (:208) and fsyncs the directory last
   (:212); if only the fsync fails it returns an error although the new index is
   already live, and Create then removes the content file — Get 200s, Open 404s
   "index entry without content". Fix: treat post-rename fsyncDir error as
   non-fatal (durability-only, log it), or have Create distinguish pre-rename from
   post-rename failure and only remove content when the rename did not happen.
   Trigger requires a directory-fsync failure — vanishingly rare on Linux.
3. Crash windows leave orphans with no reconciliation (nit): crash between content
   rename and index save leaves an unreachable orphan content file, never reaped;
   crash mid-Delete leaves an index entry pointing at missing content. No startup
   sweep exists. Fix (follow-up): on New/first load, drop index entries whose
   content file is missing (or log them) and remove unindexed content files; at
   minimum document the behaviour.
4. Multipart temp files land in os.TempDir() (/tmp, 512M tmpfs in this VM), not
   the data dir (nit): ParseMultipartForm buffers the whole body to parser temp
   files before the handler streams it to the store, so an upload near the 512 MiB
   cap can fail at parse time even with data-dir space available. Fix: stream with
   multipart.Reader directly, or document the TMPDIR requirement.
5. Routes test accepts "chat" OR "models" (nit, test-strength): TestFilesRoutesProposeScopes
   would pass if the table proposed the wrong scope; the table itself is correct
   (all five "chat"). Fix: require exactly "chat".
6. Observation (not a defect): no collision check on generated ids — 12 crypto/rand
   bytes (96-bit entropy), never checked against the index; a collision would
   silently overwrite another file's content and duplicate the id. Probability
   ~2^-96 per pair — negligible and acceptable — but a one-line check-and-regenerate
   under the lock would close it for free.
7. Acknowledged open limits (not defects): no pagination, no expiry, no per-purpose
   file-type validation, status always "processed" — all stated in code comments.
   Pagination must land before FULL.
Registration snippet (verified complete/correct): guard rows after the Responses
rows (guard.go:165-168), descriptions verbatim from FilesRoutes(); server.go New()
after line 1330, all five point at the single dispatcher s.handleFiles (dispatches
on path + method, 405 for wrong methods). register() builds method-prefixed
patterns so POST+GET /v1/files and GET+DELETE /v1/files/{id} sharing a path do not
conflict; {id} matches a single segment, so /v1/files/{id} vs /v1/files/{id}/content
are distinct. TierManagement correct (not TierLocalOnly — files paths match no
spawn-capable pattern). Coordinator may append "Gateway-key auth required." to the
descriptions, matching the Responses rows.
PARITY: agree MISSING → PARTIAL, not FULL (pagination missing; expiry + .jsonl
validation also missing/documented). Scope: agree chat for all five (Responses
stored-object precedent, guard.go:165-168).
UPLOAD-SIZE VERDICT: SET A CAP — 512 MiB per file. Keep-uncapped is the wrong call
for a gateway: any key carrying the chat scope could fill the disk with one
unbounded upload (availability risk); "no generic limit exists" is an absence of
precedent, not a design decision. The value is not invented: the OpenAI reference
states "Individual files can be up to 512 MB". Implement as a named const in
files_api.go (filesMaxUploadBytes = 512 << 20), enforced with http.MaxBytesReader
before ParseMultipartForm, 413 on overflow. Per-purpose tightening (Batch/fine-tune
.jsonl 200 MB) can follow in M8.8. Land at merge — ~5 lines, do not defer.

## M8.5 review — FAIL (one blocking defect) (2026-11-19 ~00:24 IST)

Independent review of the images passthrough slice (builder 25 tests). Reviewer
re-ran 25/25 PASS (provider 5/5, router 6/6, server 14/14), go build exit 0,
gofmt clean, toggle proof re-run PASS (supportsImages forced false via overlay →
verbatim + native-skip tests FAIL with NoCapableProviderError; live file
byte-identical after). No forbidden file touched. /tmp 31% used at review time —
builder's 95% space caveat is stale.
BLOCKING FINDING 1 — malformed upstream 200 answered as HTTP 200:
internal/provider/images_client.go PostImage returns
&UpstreamError{StatusCode: http.StatusOK, Body: "malformed images response: ..."}
on a 200 with an unparseable body; internal/server/images.go handleImagesEngineError
writes ue.StatusCode verbatim — 200 passes its 100–599 range check, so the client
gets HTTP 200 + Content-Type: application/json + a plain-text error body.
Live-proven with an overlay-only probe (fake engine returning that exact error):
handler status = 200, body = "malformed images response: unexpected end of JSON
input". Telemetry simultaneously logs upstream_5xx (statusForEngineError → 502),
so the wire says 200 while telemetry says 502. Realistic trigger: a large b64_json
response truncated at the 10 MiB maxResponseBody cap fails json.Unmarshal and takes
exactly this path — the "honest malformed" failure is a dishonest success. The
embeddings precedent does not have this hole (handleEmbedEngineError delegates
non-NCP errors to handleEngineError → 502); images' own interceptor bypasses it.
FIX (one line in each of two places + regression test): in handleImagesEngineError,
clamp status < 400 to 502, AND set StatusCode: http.StatusBadGateway in the PostImage
malformed branch (today its Error() string also reads "upstream error: HTTP 200" in
logs). Add a regression test pinning 502 for a malformed upstream 200. Must land
before merge. Fix agent spawned (see below).
Other findings (nits): 2. Plugin model rewrite mismatch (generations): plugin
rewrites the JSON body to model B but imgReq.Model (and bookReq.Model, restored to
A) stay at A — engine resolves the chain for A while upstream serves B. Embeddings
precedent syncs the rewritten model onto the real request. Fix: for generations,
set model = bookReq.Model after the rewrite (before building imgReq). 3. Taxonomy
gap (confirmed): wireName() has no EndpointImages case; RetryableStatusForEndpoint
treats 405/501 as retryable only for responses/embeddings/rerank, so images 405/501
classify under the chat rule. Fix (coordinator, shared files): add case
EndpointImages → "images" to wireName() + "images" to the 405/501 list. Impact
limited (exactly-once either way; only breaker/cooldown attribution + reason slug).
4. Truncation vs malformed indistinguishable: doForEndpoint silently truncates at
10 MiB via LimitReader; PostImage cannot tell a truncated body from garbage. Fix:
detect a read hitting the cap, return a distinct "upstream response exceeded 10 MiB"
error (still 502 after Finding 1's fix). 5. "Unknown when unreported" only half
true: reconcileUsage substitutes the local prompt-text estimate (LocalEstimate=true)
into the ledger row when upstream reports zero usage — telemetry headers stay absent
(correct), but the ledger row carries an estimate, not a bare unknown. Same shared
machinery as embeddings; fix the claim in the report, or leave the behaviour.
6. Test gaps: no test pins (a) malformed-upstream-200 → 502 (Finding 1 regression),
(b) 429 Retry-After header forwarding end-to-end at server level, (c) plugin model
rewrite on generations. Add all three. 7. Edits has no stream rejection (stream
multipart field forwarded untouched; generations refuses stream:true 400) — acceptable,
OpenAI edits has no stream param; note the asymmetry. 8. Mixed-spelling usage edge:
upstream reporting input_tokens: 0 + output_tokens: 5 alongside prompt_tokens: 7 —
input branch wins, prompt maps to 0, dropping the reported 7. Pathological mixed
report; documented "input wins" rule covers it. Leave or tighten to per-field fallback.
Verified claims: endpoints externally verified (OpenAI reference: variations
"retired and no longer available"; exactly two live Images endpoints; /upscale does
NOT exist in OpenAI's API — exists only in third-party OpenAI-compatible servers
LocalAI/Lemonade/Osaurus/Doop). No raw-POST seam confirmed (provider.Client is
exactly Chat/ChatStream/ListModels/TestKey, client.go:1121-1133); ImagePoster +
PostImage necessary, mirrors embed_client.go. Engine path confirmed (Engine.Images →
singleAttempt, embeddings.go:248; chain ordering, skips, budget gate, limiter shed,
ledger, trace with markExempt stamping exempt:images; projection enforced via
checkEndpointStrategy, embeddings.go:282; native skip before dialling proven by test;
all-native → NoCapableProviderError → handler 400). Handler mirror confirmed
item-by-item vs handleEmbedRerank. Edits multipart forwarded byte-for-byte
(sniffMultipartFields reads only the in-memory body copy). Upstream errors carry
status + body verbatim; 429 → RateLimitError with Retry-After forwarded (code-verified,
header-first with body fallback). stream:true → 400 on generations. Usage bug fix
confirmed (input_tokens spelling selected only when InputTokens != 0 || OutputTokens

Independent review of the Responses sub-actions slice (builder 6 tests). Reviewer

tests + 6 reviewer probes, no race), go vet exit 0, gofmt clean. NO overlay needed
for the M84 or regression runs — the sibling-churn compile failures in the

review (probes recreated each time); GOTMPDIR=$HOME/.gotmp worked throughout.
Probes ran only via -overlay from /tmp; git status confirms no probe file entered
the tree. Toggle proof, real (reviewer's own probe): bare mux with no handler →
404; same request on the wired mux → 200. The builder's
TestM84InputItemsToggleFails only proves CheckResponse rejects a wrong status —
it never removes the handler (see nit 6).
Semantics verified against real code: transcript-minus-final-assistant-turn is
correct, always (buildResponsesObject responses.go:854 always returns an assistant
message; storeResponsesRecord :935-942 appends exactly one, for non-stream,
stream-terminal, and finishFailed paths alike, for completed/incomplete/failed
alike). A tool-call-only final turn drops its calls too — correct, they are this
response's output. A continuation whose base ends in an assistant turn keeps that
turn (only the last is dropped); the converter's msgs[last].Role == "assistant"
guard means nothing is dropped if the last message ever isn't assistant. Ids are
stable (positional, slice order, no map iteration). Parts survive the transcript
round-trip (Message.MarshalJSON serialises parts as a content array; UnmarshalJSON
rebuilds them), so the input_image branch is live, not dead code. Concurrency: no
race (Store.Get returns a struct copy under mutex; Put replaces entries wholesale).
Cancel MISSING-by-design confirmed: statuses exactly completed/incomplete/failed/
in_progress (internal/responses/responses.go:48-51); no cancel func, no in-flight
registry, no cancelled status anywhere (only cancel in responses.go is a
request-scoped context.WithTimeout). Extra support: OpenAI cancel applies only to
background:true responses and this gateway has no background mode. No GET
output-items endpoint confirmed against the fetched OpenAI reference (endpoints:
create/get/delete/cancel/compact/input_items/input_tokens + websocket connect).
input_tokens out of scope confirmed (OmniRoute has responses/input_tokens/route.ts;
PARITY:1041 lists token-count MISSING; m8.3 fixtures exist). [...path] unproxyable
confirmed — OmniRoute's src/app/api/v1/responses/[...path]/route.ts is a POST
catch-all forwarding arbitrary sub-paths "all the way to the upstream provider" via
Codex passthrough; this gateway translates Responses to chat (responsesChatRequest +
engine Chat) and the provider client has no upstream Responses wire path (only the
taxonomy label). Instructions gap confirmed real (responses.go:1204 transcriptBase
excludes instructions; responsesChatRequest adds them only to the engine request) —
arguably OpenAI-faithful, since instructions is a response-level field, not an
input item.
Findings (7 nits + 1 observation, all non-blocking):
1. responses_items.go:219 — 409 conflates "in progress" with "terminal record,
   transcript missing": rec.Status == StatusInProgress || len(rec.TranscriptJSON)
   == 0 answers 409 "still in progress" for a COMPLETED record whose transcript
   is empty (probe confirms: resp_empty, completed, no transcript → 409).
   Unreachable via production store paths (storeResponsesRecord always writes a
   transcript; only the placeholder lacks one and is always in_progress). Fix:
   split the checks — in_progress → 409; terminal + empty transcript → 500
   "stored response transcript is missing".
2. responses_items.go:3-13 — header inventory omits POST /v1/responses/compact.
   The fetched OpenAI reference (2026-11-19) lists "Compact conversation — POST
   /responses/compact" alongside input_items/input_tokens. "Exactly two sub-actions
   on one stored response" is still literally true (compact is top-level), but the
   inventory reads as a complete surface list. Fix: add one line noting compact
   exists as a separate top-level endpoint for the compaction slice (M7), same
   treatment as input_tokens.
3. responses_items.go:234-275 — include query param silently ignored. OpenAI's
   input_items accepts include (e.g. message.input_image.image_url,
   reasoning.encrypted_content). The gateway returns image URLs unconditionally and
   holds no encrypted reasoning, so there is nothing to include — but a client
   passing include= gets no signal. Fix (optional): note in the handler comment
   that include is accepted-and-ignored.
4. responses_items.go:168-172 + :143-146 — "pass through verbatim" overstates.
   The unknown-part default branch carries only Type+Text (an ImageURL on an
   unknown part type is dropped), and tool-role messages drop Parts entirely
   (Output: m.Content only). In practice stored tool messages carry Content only
   and unknown part types are hypothetical. Fix: carry ImageURL through in the
   default branch too, or soften the comment to "type + text".
5. responses_items.go:50-52 — "Pagination mirrors GET /v1/responses" is inaccurate
   on order. handleListResponses (responses.go:1349-1351) is desc-only (any other
   order → 400); input_items accepts asc (default) + desc. Behaviour is fine and
   both orders work (desc+after probe passes); fix the comment. Also: reviewer
   could NOT verify OpenAI's order default from the fetched reference text — the TS
   page lists {after, include, limit, order} with no defaults shown (the "default
   desc" seen was for the Conversations items endpoint, a different endpoint). The
   asc default is consistent with the fixture; treat as unverified, not wrong.
6. responses_items_test.go — toggle test is weak. TestM84InputItemsToggleFails only
   proves CheckResponse rejects status 201 vs 200; it never runs the fixture
   against a handler-less mux. Reviewer proved the real toggle (above). Fix
   (optional): add a fixture run against a mux without the route, expecting failure.
7. Fixtures responses-input-items-404.json / -409.json: recorded_response.status is
   200. Harmless (apitest only checks assert), explicitly labelled a placeholder,
   but inconsistent with m8.3's 404 fixture (status 404). Fix for tidiness: set
   them to 404/409.
8. Observation (not a defect) — reasoning items also absent. Same class as the
   documented instructions gap: provider.Message.Reasoning is never marshalled
   (client.go MarshalJSON never sets reasoning_content), so a prior turn's
   reasoning item can never appear in input_items. Worth one line in the header
   comment alongside instructions.

requests → chat 429 key_pool_limit, 0 engine calls). Toggle proofs re-run and
passing. go test ./internal/apitest/ ok; go vet clean; gofmt clean. Stale caveat:

tests now; files_api.go:185 and images.go:421 show no error. Sibling builders

during the review window — checked, they are other slices' review entries and

Reuse claims all confirmed: handlers use ledger.Team / config.TeamConfig /
validateTeamUpdate (teams.go:272) / teamStatus (teams.go:44); pools use
ledger.QuotaPool / validateQuotaPoolUpdate (quotapools.go:243) /

quotaPoolView / teamsResponse / quotaPoolsResponse structs (apiTeamView /
apiQuotaPoolView mirror the dashboard view builders field-for-field, incl. the
0.5 saturation default). Ordering is ledger-first, then config persist with
rollback, mirroring handleTeamUpdate / handleQuotaPoolUpdate. DELETE rollback limit
is real and disclosed in code (teams_api.go:550-560, pools_api.go:385-394: row
restored, bindings closed by the delete are gone). PATCH budget-clear resets the
window exactly like the dashboard (teams_api.go:470-490 vs teams.go:388-396;
normalizeTeam re-anchors the window). GET-side window advance is pre-existing
dashboard behaviour (teams.go:185), not a new API side effect.
Findings (9, all nits):
1. NEW shares rule invents a constraint the engine does not have — RELAX IT
   (coordinator decision). pools_api.go:99-113 (validateAPIQuotaPoolShares): with
   ≥1 allocation, weights must sum > 0 and ≤ 100. Evidence from the enforcement
   path, ledger/pool.go:242: fairShare := alloc.Weight / 100 * effectiveLimit is
   computed PER ALLOCATION, independently; the global ceiling (total >=
   effectiveLimit) binds regardless of the sum. Sum 120 (60+60) does not break
   anything — each member is just promised a larger fair share, and the global cap
   still caps the pool. All-zero weights are likewise meaningful, not "no share to
   enforce": a weight-0 hard member has fair share 0 and is blocked once the pool
   passes the saturation threshold (and a weight-0 burst member can still borrow
   below the global cap). The existing validator (quotapools.go:294-295) already
   allows per-allocation weights 0–100, and the DASHBOARD ACCEPTS POOLS THIS API
   REJECTS — a pool creatable via POST /dashboard/quota-pools gets a 400 from POST
   /api/quota-pools, violating the slice's own "API and dashboard can never
   disagree" principle. Fix: remove the two 400s (keep the dashboard as the single
   source of truth for pool validity); if the coordinator wants the strictness, add
   the rule to the shared validateQuotaPoolUpdate instead so both surfaces agree,
   and say so in the file header. Non-blocking (deliberate, documented, tested,
   fail-closed) — but must not ship silently as an API-only divergence. Reviewer
   verdict: relax to warning / remove the 400 (no warnings channel in the response
   shape, so the practical action is removal; guardrails belong in the shared
   validator or behind a warnings field, not as an API-only 400).
2. PATCH teams can leave a half-applied membership change with no rollback.
   teams_api.go:504-513: the unassign loop runs first, then the assign loop; an
   assign failure returns 400 with the raw ledger error after SetTeam already
   persisted and some members were already unassigned — ledger/config diverge and
   the caller gets a misleading 400 for what is really a ledger/DB failure. Same
   shape at :504-505 (unassign failure → 500 after SetTeam). Realistically rare
   (AssignKeyToTeam moves keys rather than failing on cross-team membership, so
   failure needs a DB error or an archived-race), and the persist-failure rollback
   path right below it is best-effort-disclosed — but this mid-mutation path has NO
   rollback and NO disclosure. Fix: on member-sync failure, attempt the same
   best-effort restore to prevIDs used in persistAPITeamConfig, and return 500 (not
   400 with a ledger:-prefixed message) for ledger errors.
3. Duplicate-create 409 is racy. teams_api.go:183-187, pools_api.go:180-183:
   check-then-act (GetTeam/GetQuotaPool) over an upsert (SetTeam/SetQuotaPool are
   ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE). Two concurrent creates with the same id both pass the
   check and both answer 201, the second overwriting the first. Same pattern as the
   dashboard handlers, single-operator local tool — acceptable, but the 409 contract
   is not atomic. Fix (if wanted): existence check + insert in one transaction, or
   accept the race and note it.
4. Auto-generated id collision answers 409 instead of retrying. teams_api.go:126-127
   (newAPIID: 4 bytes = 32 bits of crypto/rand): a collision with an existing id
   falls into the duplicate path and returns 409 to the caller. Probability ~1 in
   4 billion per attempt, but a server-generated id should never 409. Fix: on
   collision, regenerate (a few retries) instead of returning 409.
5. Pools PATCH has no explicit-null semantics, unlike teams PATCH. pools_api.go:308-340
   decodes into pointer fields, so {"name": null} / {"dimensions": null} silently
   keep stored values, while teams PATCH (teams_api.go:376+, apiTeamPatchField)
   treats null as clear. The pools behaviour matches the dashboard's pointer
   semantics, so it is not wrong — but the two resources in one slice now disagree,
   and a caller clearing a pool field via null gets a silent no-op 200. Fix:
   document the asymmetry in the pools file header, or add the same explicit-null
   handling.
6. PATCH status: null silently reactivates an archived team. teams_api.go PATCH nulls
   next.Status to "", and ledger/team.go normalizeTeam defaults "" → "active". So
   {"status": null} on an archived team reopens it — probably not what the caller
   meant by "clear". Fix: reject explicit-null status with a 400, or document that
   null status means "active".
7. Create/PATCH silently steals members from other teams. ledger/team.go:266-311:
   AssignKeyToTeam closes the key's existing binding on another team and opens a
   new one — it never fails for cross-team membership. That is the ledger's own rule
   and the dashboard does the same, so it is not a slice defect; but the API
   response never surfaces the steal. Fix: at minimum note it in the file header (a
   members list that includes a key currently on another team will move that key);
   optionally return the previous team in the response.
8. Builder's test count is wrong (report correction, not a code defect). Claimed
   "13 test functions PASS"; the files contain 12 (grep -c "^func TestAPI": teams
   7, pools 5), and both reviewer runs show 12 --- PASS lines. All 12 pass; the
   count in the slice report should say 12.
9. Cosmetic: pools_api.go:165 create decodes quotaPoolUpdate, which carries a Delete
   flag — POST /api/quota-pools {"delete": true} silently creates a pool instead of
   being rejected. Also every fixture's recorded_response.status is 200 even on the
   201 fixtures; harmless (internal/apitest/fixture.go:195-196 only validity-checks
   it; Run drives the real mux), but sloppy. Fix by ignoring/validating Delete on
   create and setting recorded_response.status to match assert.status.

handleAPIQuotaPools/handleAPIQuotaPool (exact lines in the review handoff).

## M8.15 review — FAIL, one blocking defect (2026-11-19 ~00:32 IST)
Independent review verdict: FAIL. Slice otherwise solid — every claimed behaviour the reviewer could check verified, all tests pass in the reviewer's own run, both toggle proofs reproduce — but concurrent PATCH requests race on the price-book maps, proven with -race in the shipped handler.
Reviewer runs (live tree, no overlay needed): go build ./internal/server/ ./internal/apitest/ clean; gofmt clean on all 4 new Go files; go test -count=1 -v -run 'TestM815' ./internal/server/ — 17/17 top-level PASS (+4 subtests under TestM815PricingPatchUnknownIDs = 21 PASS lines), ~1.1s. CORRECTION to builder claim: 17 tests, not 18 (11 TestM815* in pricing_api_test.go, 6 in cache_mgmt_test.go). Builder's sibling-breakage caveat stale: webhooks_api_test.go compiles fine now. Toggle 1 (wrong-status fixture fails) confirmed; toggle 2 (cache-delete fixture fails against unseeded caches) reproduced via overlay probe: fixture fails against unseeded caches, evicted.exact got 0 want 2 — counts come from the real flush.
F1 — BLOCKING: data race on the price-book maps between PATCH validation (unlocked) and PATCH apply (locked). pricing_api.go:364 runs validatePricingOverride BEFORE pricingMu.Lock(); validation reads the book via pricingPairKnown → PriceBook.LookupPair (pricing_api.go:576, internal/config/prices.go:356). Apply writes s.priceBook.Pairs[key] = merged under the lock (pricing_api.go:424). -race output (2 warnings, same shape): Write runtime.mapassign handleAPIPricingPatch pricing_api.go:424 vs Previous read runtime.mapaccess2 PriceBook.LookupPair → pricingPairKnown pricing_api.go:576 → validatePricingOverride pricing_api.go:536 → handleAPIPricingPatch pricing_api.go:364. In a non-race build this interleaving is a fatal concurrent map read and map write — the whole gateway process crashes. Also falsifies the file header's claim that pricingMu serialises GET snapshots against PATCH applies so the two endpoints never race — GET is safe, PATCH-vs-PATCH is not. FIX (within owned files): move the validation loop inside the pricingMu critical section (acquire the lock before validation; the 503 check on s.cfg/configPersist can stay where it is). Ledger-map reads in validation are safe (reference swap under ledgerPricesMu, map never mutated in place) — only the book maps race. configPersist already called under the lock, so the saves counter is serialized.
F2 — Nit: builder's "18/18" count is wrong; actual 17 top-level (21 PASS lines incl. subtests).
F3 — Nit: DELETE notes leak internal repo paths/type names into an operator-facing response (cache_mgmt.go: semantic note names internal/cache/semantic_persist.go; reasoning note names internal/degrade/degrade.go and cache.ReasoningCache). Fix: generic wording, keep detail in the file header.
F4 — Nit: GET list-mode ?provider= filter inconsistent between the two table paths. Ledger path (apiPricingTable) includes model-level rows (empty provider) even with a provider filter; book-only path excludes model rows when a filter is set. Ledger path also omits half-known book pairs from list mode while single-pair lookup shows them. Fix: pick one rule, apply to both (suggest including model-level fallback rows under a provider filter), document list mode is the ledger-effective table.
F5 — Nit: PATCH validation never consults the model-level fallback, so a pair GET shows as priced via fallback can be un-PATCHable on book-only servers. pricingPairKnown checks LookupPair/LookupSyncedPair/ledger but never LookupModel. Fix: treat LookupModel(model) + known provider as pair-known, or document the refusal.
F6 — Nit: misleading fixture metadata — tests/fixtures/v1/m8.15/pricing-patch-400-invalid-rate.json has recorded_response.status 200 while assert.status 400. Fix: set to 400.
F7 — Nit: exact-layer engine branch untested. fakeEngine implements no FlushStore, so tests exercise only the exactCacheFlushStore adapter path; the production path (degrade wrapper via cache.FlushStore) is never driven by DELETE in tests. Fix: add a fake engine implementing cache.FlushStore asserting DELETE routes through it.
Verified by reviewer: scope — 8 new untracked files only, no forbidden file touched (the 3 modified tracked files in the shared tree — docs/LOG.md, internal/router/cascade.go, internal/server/internalbudgets.go — are other slices' work); fixtures all "fixture": true, mock pair/rates only, no invented prices/models/caps; GET returns ledger table with per-row source, unpriced pair → priced:false, no rates, empty source; PATCH three-write + rollback verified (refreshLedgerPricesFromBookLocked conversion matches buildLedgerPrices main.go:1214; rollback snapshots all three; SetLedgerPrices(nil) restores "no ledger prices"; configPersist() is the only fallible step after apply and its error branch restores all three); all-or-nothing validation (invalid-rate 400 names input_usd_per_1m, 0 saves, GET unchanged); 503 path real (s.cfg == nil || s.configPersist == nil → 503 before any mutation); zero rates valid (config.ModelPrice.Validate, prices.go:84); DELETE real per-layer counts, pricing JSON identical before/after, second DELETE evicts 0 with entries_before 0 — honest (0-because-empty distinguishable from 0-because-unflushable via flushed:false + note); limitation (1) reasoning verified (zero FlushReasoningCache implementations outside cache_mgmt.go; StaleEngine.ReasoningCacheStats exists degrade.go:320; wireReasoningCache wires cache onto wrapper only main.go:1636; ## M8.9 review — FAIL, one blocking defect + one blocking registration correction (2026-11-19 ~00:33 IST)
Independent review verdict: FAIL — one blocking defect in the handler itself, plus one blocking correction to the proposed registration. Everything else checked verified clean; this is a small-fix FAIL, not a rebuild. Reviewer did not build the slice, edited no live-tree file, never pushed/committed, no remote (git remote -v empty; HEAD b900f4d).
Reviewer runs (clean temp worktree at HEAD — live tree could not be tested directly, sibling untracked files present per the builder's caveat; reviewer did NOT independently re-verify the main-tree compile errors; first worktree under /tmp was wiped by a runtime service restart mid-review, recreated at ~/.tmp/m89-review, since removed; live tree untouched): gofmt clean on both new files; go vet ./internal/server/ exit 0; go test -count=1 -v -run TestModerations ./internal/server/ — 11/11 PASS (3 fixture contracts PII/clean/injection, toggle, secret, array, text-parts, no-echo, validation 6 subtests, 405, bare-nil-store — builder's 11/11 confirmed); go test -count=1 ./internal/server/ ./internal/apitest/ ./internal/guardrails/ — all ok (internal/server ok, 461s wall time inflated by heavy concurrent sibling load, load avg ~6–8; builder's "91s" not reproduced, but pass/fail is what matters and it passed; apitest ok 0.272s; guardrails ok 0.503s). Reviewer's own 6 probe tests (scratch file in temp worktree only, deleted with it) — all pass: hate/violence/bomb-instructions not detected; arbitrary model echo; ["pii"]-scoped key skipping secret/injection at handler level; input-shape edges; over-cap body; applied-input-types shape. Toggle proof re-run and confirmed: TestModerationsFixtureToggle asserts CheckResponse FAILS with wrong status 201 and FAILS expecting pii:false for a PII input, and the positive contract passes against the same response.
F1 — BLOCKING: category_applied_input_types is the wrong JSON type — not OpenAI-shaped. moderations.go:128 (CategoryAppliedInputTypes []string) and :238 (populated as []string{"text"}). Verified against OpenAI's live API reference (platform.openai.com/docs/api-reference/moderations/create, crawled 2026-11-19): category_applied_input_types is a PER-CATEGORY OBJECT, e.g. {"harassment": ["text"], "sexual": ["text", "image"], "violence": ["text", "image"], …} — one entry per category. Official SDKs type it as a dict; a JSON array fails strict response validation, so the endpoint breaks for standard OpenAI clients on every response. FIX: replace the field with a struct/map carrying all 13 category keys, each ["text"] (local engine is text-only), and extend the fixture assertions to pin the object shape. Also correct the doc comment at moderations.go:10 and the slice report, which both present ["text"] as the OpenAI shape.
F2 — BLOCKING (registration, not slice code): proposed scope "models" is wrong — registration must use "chat". Builder's premise factually inverted. In guard.go, embeddings (:162) and rerank (:163) — the cited precedents — both carry Scopes: ["chat"], as do all 8 compute endpoints. "models" appears only on 2 GET read-only listings: GET /v1/models (:160) and GET /v1/auto-combo/{channel}/candidates (:171). requireScope (server.go:1478–1506) answers 403 to a key lacking the listed scope (or admin), so registering moderations under ["models"] would 403 every normal chat-scoped key and would make it the first POST compute endpoint behind the read-only-listing scope. FIX (coordinator at registration): register with Scopes: ["chat"]. Exact snippet: guard.go TierManagement section after the rerank row (~line 163): {Method: "POST", Path: "/v1/moderations", Tier: TierManagement, Description: "Moderations — OpenAI-shaped, backed ONLY by the router's local guardrails (PII / secret / injection patterns); no upstream call, no input stored or echoed. The 13 OpenAI categories are not judged (all false, score 0); the real verdict is in results[].local_guardrails and results[].flagged reflects it. Gateway-key auth required.", Scopes: []string{"chat"}}; server.go register block after the rerank line (~line 1325): s.register(mux, "POST", "/v1/moderations", s.handleModerations). Do NOT use the builder's proposed Scopes: ["models"].
F3 — Nit: doc comment contradicts code on empty-string arrays. moderations.go:243–250: comment claims "an array of only empty strings" is a 400, but code only rejects an empty array — probe-verified ["",""] and [""] both return 200 with clean results. FIX: correct the comment to match the code (empty strings inside a non-empty array moderate clean), or add the rejection if 400 was intended.
F4 — Nit: over-cap body returns 400, not 413. moderations.go:172–176 returns 400 "request body too large" for any MaxBytesReader error. count_tokens.go:361–368 shows the newer codebase convention: MaxBytesError → 413 RequestEntityTooLarge, other read errors → 400. (Matches the older images.go:134–138 pattern, so convention drift, not a lone error.) FIX: distinguish with errors.As as count_tokens.go does.
F5 — Nit: malformed-JSON error leaks a Go type name. Probe-verified: a top-level array body returns "invalid request: json: cannot unmarshal array into Go value of type server.moderationRequest". FIX: return a generic message without err.Error() for unmarshal failures.
F6 — Nit (disclosure, not a defect): moderation traffic pollutes chat guardrail metrics. Each call goes through Store.CheckScoped, which increments the shared store's checked/byKind/blocked counters and appends an in-memory Event with a redacted-text preview (guardrails.go:1161–1172). Raw input is never stored (preview is the redacted text), but moderation scans will inflate the chat PhaseRequest dashboard counts, and a PII moderation input increments the store's "blocked" counter even though the handler ignores res.Blocked and blocks nothing. FIX (optional): document the mixing in LOG/PARITY, or consider a non-recording scan path later.
F7 — Nit, no change demanded: echoing a real upstream model id is mildly misleading. Probe-verified: model "omni-moderation-latest" and "totally-fake-model-xyz" echoed verbatim. A client requesting a real OpenAI moderation model id gets it back with local verdicts. Mitigation already in place: local_only: true and the notes field on every response. Keep the echo (standard gateway behaviour); no fix required.
Verified by reviewer: new files only, fixtures mock-labelled (moderations.go, moderations_test.go, 3 fixtures under tests/fixtures/v1/m8.9/, all "fixture": true, provider mock-upstream, no live data). No forbidden file touched (guard.go, server.go, config.go, docs/PARITY.md, docs/PLAN.md, docs/CHECKLIST.md, README.md, README.v2.md, web/index.html all clean in git status/diff; docs/LOG.md "M" at end of review is a sibling's M8.14 review entry appended ~00:17 IST, not M8.9's). Guardrails integration is the real thing: handler calls store.CheckScoped(text, guardrails.PhaseRequest, allowed) with allowed := s.keyGuardrailScope(r) (moderations.go:197, :193) — identical call shape to chat's applyGuardrails (server.go:4575). Same function, same PhaseRequest constant, same scoping helper. No upstream call, no echo, no storage: handler has zero engine/provider/log/ledger/recorder references (only prose comments). guardrails.Finding (guardrails.go:203–207) carries Kind/Pattern/Count only; scan() uses FindAllStringIndex (indices, never matched substrings). TestModerationsNoInputEcho and the secret test pass. Category mapping verified against internal/guardrails: PII = email/phone/card/Aadhaar (guardrails.go:480–483); secret = api-key/password/bearer/provider keys/JWT (:459–478); injection = 11 rules, counted — ignore, disregard, forget, you-are-now, reveal, print, override, bypass, jailbreak, dan-mode, new-instructions (:496–519). "No OpenAI category covers PII" is correct: response emits exactly OpenAI's 13 categories (hate, hate/threatening, harassment, harassment/threatening, illicit, illicit/violent, self-harm, self-harm/intent, self-harm/instructions, sexual, sexual/minors, violence, violence/graphic — cross-checked against OpenAI's docs), none of which is PII. All 13 stay false with score 0 in every response — only path that sets them is the zero-value struct in moderationResultFor (moderations.go:219–241); scores are 0/1 only. Limitations verified by probe: (a) hate, violence, bomb-instructions inputs all return flagged: false — confirmed; (b) per-key scoping applied AND proven at handler level through the real key path using the existing scopedTestServer helper: a ["pii"]-scoped key does NOT flag secret or injection inputs but DOES flag PII; unscoped key flags all three. checkFiltered (guardrails.go:1118–1132) filters findings by f.Kind for all three kinds, so the scoping claim holds for every kind; (c) no model validation — arbitrary model strings echoed verbatim (see nit 7). Input shapes probed: mixed array ["hello", {"type":"text",…}] → clean 400; single object (not array) → 400; input: true → 400; top-level array body → 400; image_url part → 400 (existing test). No panics, no silent drops anywhere. ID modr-local-<UnixNano> — distinct across all probe calls, cannot collide with real modr-<hex> ids. Bare-nil-store test proves &Server{} doesn't panic via the moderationStore() fallback; production New() always wires a store, so test-only robustness, as claimed. Concurrency: handler stateless (per-call results slice); store mutex-guarded internally. No shared mutable state found.
Flagged semantics — KEEP as-is (reviewer verdict): results[].flagged is true on a local finding while all 13 OpenAI categories read false, which breaks OpenAI's own invariant (flagged == OR of categories). Reviewer considered flipping flagged to always false with the verdict only in local_guardrails and rejected it: that would make the endpoint answer "clean" to every plain OpenAI client, rendering the standard shape useless — the local verdict would be visible only to clients that know the Codvian extension. Current design is the only one where a plain client gets any signal, and the disclosure is real and in-band: notes (top-level, on every response) states exactly what was and wasn't judged and where the real verdict lives, plus local_only: true and the local_guardrails extension. A client routing on categories will allow PII/secret/injection content — but it is routing on categories the endpoint honestly reports as unjudged (false, score 0), which is the correct statement about those categories. No evidence mainstream SDKs validate flagged == OR(categories) cross-field consistency (they validate field types — which is precisely why finding 1 is blocking and this is not). KEEP, but record the invariant break in the PARITY row note at merge.
Scope — "chat", not "models": evidence above (finding 2). Builder's "same family as embeddings/rerank" premise points the wrong way: that family carries "chat". The Responses stored-object API also carries "chat" (guard.go:165–168). "models" in this codebase means read-only model listings only.
PARITY: AGREE — PARTIAL, not FULL. Shape + local verdict are real, but OmniRoute's upstream moderation categories are not reproduced, and hate/violence/sexual/self-harm/illicit content is not detected at all. After finding 1 is fixed, the row at docs/PARITY.md:1021 (POST /v1/moderations | HAVE | — | MISSING) should flip to PARTIAL with a note recording the category_applied_input_types fix and the flagged-semantics invariant break. Builder correctly did not edit docs/PARITY.md itself.
Fix agent spawned for F1 (blocking) + F3, F4, F5. F2 is coordinator registration work (scope chat, not models). F6 optional documentation at merge. F7 no change. Fix must prove with TestModerations 11/11+ green + apitest + build/vet/gofmt, plus a test pinning the 13-key object shape.

## M8.21 builder — DONE, re-dispatch with fixes (2026-11-19 ~00:36 IST)
Re-dispatch builder report received. Verdict: the previous attempt's files existed but were broken — all 5 of its tests FAILED on arrival. Builder fixed the defects (in those same new files only) and all 5 now PASS. Nothing rewritten from scratch; nothing pushed, committed, or remoted. HEAD still b900f4d.
Baseline before fixes — all 5 tests failed: TestAPIReferenceCoversEveryRoute, TestLLMTxtCoversEveryRoute, TestTryItRoutesMatchGuardTable, TestTryItFixtureMatchesGuardTable, TestOpenAPIGapIsDocumented.
Root causes, all in scripts/gen-api-docs.py (previous attempt's generator) and one in the test: (1) Tiers emitted as Go identifiers, not values. Generator captured TierManagement / TierLocalOnly / TierAlwaysProtected from guard.go, but RouteTable() exposes the constant values MANAGEMENT / LOCAL_ONLY / ALWAYS_PROTECTED. Consequence: doc header claimed 0 LOCAL_ONLY, 0 ALWAYS_PROTECTED, 0 MANAGEMENT; every section, llm.txt line, try-it JSON entry and fixture row carried the wrong tier string; try-it page's r.tier === "LOCAL_ONLY" auth label could never fire. (2) All 50 scopes silently dropped. Generator's per-entry tail regex stopped at the first } — the closing brace of Scopes: []string{...} — so no scope ever parsed. Every scope-gated route was documented as "any authenticated key" / scopes=-. True counts: 50 scope-gated (admin 40, chat 8, models 2), 56 unscoped. (3) Descriptions not unescaped. \"disabled\" / \"key_id\" stayed literal in the doc and llm.txt, so the verbatim-description check failed for DELETE /admin/keys/{id} and POST /admin/cache/flush. (4) Test's gap probe was a false positive. TestOpenAPIGapIsDocumented searched the live spec body for the substring "scope" — but openapi.go uses guard descriptions verbatim as summaries, and several mention "scope" in prose (e.g. "(admin scope)", "name, scopes, budget"). The gap itself is real (no structured scopes/security field); the probe was wrong.
Fixes applied (new files only): scripts/gen-api-docs.py — parse_guard_table() rewritten to parse per source line (one table entry per line), map tier identifiers to values via TIER_VALUES (unknown identifier fails loudly), parse scopes from the full line, and JSON-decode Go string escapes so descriptions are verbatim. Gap-note wording refined to "no *structured* scope information" (prose mentions noted). ## M8.10 review — PASS-WITH-NITS (2026-11-19 ~00:38 IST)
Independent review (re-dispatch reviewer) verdict: PASS-WITH-NITS. Slice sound. All builder claims the reviewer could test reproduced; no blocking defect in the slice code itself. One must-fix item lives in the PROPOSED REGISTRATION (not the handler code): the {model} pattern cannot serve the virtual models the list itself advertises. Reviewer runs (live tree, no scratch/overlay needed): sibling compile blockers from the builder's caveat are GONE — go vet ./internal/server/ completed in-tree in ~10s, exit 0. Builder's SIGTERM was a harness time-limit on a slow full-package run, not a slice problem — reviewer's filtered run also took ~200s wall (mostly building the huge server package). go test ./internal/server/ -run 'TestM810|TestModels' -count=1 — 14/14 M8.10 PASS, 15/15 TestModels* PASS (FilterByProvider/Category/MaxPrice/MinContext, both Shortlist tests, all 5 Refresh tests, DeprecatedFiltering, UsesConfiguredModels/UsesRegistry/UsesProviderRegistry, ListsAutoVirtualModels). No regression. go test ./internal/apitest/ -count=1 — PASS. gofmt -l on both new files — clean. Live tree untouched by reviewer: all work in /tmp/m810-review (deleted afterwards); git status shows slice files still untracked, forbidden files (server.go, guard.go, config.go, docs/PARITY.md, docs/PLAN.md, docs/CHECKLIST.md, README.md, web/index.html) unmodified. docs/LOG.md modification in the tree is another slice's work (M8.14 review entry), not M8.10's.
Toggle proofs (reproduced in scratch copy): Toggle 1 — forced detail to 404: TestM810ModelDetailFixture FAILS with "status: got 404, want 200". Assertion path is live. Toggle 2 — deleted the capability 400: TestM810ModelsQueryCapability400Fixture FAILS with "status: got 200, want 400". Assertion path is live.
Verified claims (read the real code): Sourcing mirror — m810ListModels (models_detail.go:51) is line-for-line the same sourcing as handleModels (server.go:3881): registry.Models(ctx), else WithModels fallback, plus autorouter.VirtualModels + IntentVirtualModelNames(), deprecated hidden unless included. Both call the same registry.Models, and the adapter dedups by model ID (server.go:406-439, fresh slice each call — the in-place deprecated filter cannot corrupt registry state). No drift: no model either side returns that the other misses. Detail correctly passes includeDeprecated=true (hiding is display-only, matching handleModels). Base URL never leaks — Model (server.go:319) has no URL field; modelDetailResponse adds only price + *bool base_url_configured. Probed all three endpoints' bodies: no example.com anywhere, including error paths (writeError carries only a message). Presence semantics confirmed: *bool + omitempty — nil omits the field, false is present. Primary seam providerRemover.Get returns a clone under RLock (registry.go:855); stored BaseURL is filled from the catalog at load when empty (resolveCatalogForLoad, registry.go:784-791), so the catalog-default case is not a false negative. Fallback resolver goes through RelayTargetForModel → resolveAuthHeaders → catalog.ResolveAuth, which returns ErrCredentialRequired (catalog/auth.go:20-22,130) for a credentialed endpoint with no key — confirmed real; on resolver failure presence stays nil (omitted, never guessed false). Price — resolveModelPrice (models_detail.go:109): pair → model → models.dev via existing pairPrice (server.go:2474); Known() requires any leg non-nil, BlendedUSDPer1M() is the pre-existing (in+out)/2 requiring both legs (config/prices.go:108-124) — "blended" is a real price-book concept, not invented here. Unpriced → known:false, source:"unknown", all numerics nil (pointer fields, verified by decode in test + reviewer probe). Configured $0 pair → known:true, source:"pair", blended:0 — distinguishable from unknown by known + non-null blended. One-leg provider-reported price → unknown (probed). Free-tier rule uses the same rowBlendedPriceUSDPer1M as the max_price filter — consistent. Query — probed: ?provider= matches by id AND display name, case-insensitive (Provider B, provider b, PROVIDER B → [b-model]); unknown provider → empty 200 list (matches /v1/models); malformed free-tier-only=maybe → 400 naming the parameter; free = category-free OR known blended $0, unknown never free (probed all three). ?capability= always 400 naming the gap. Capability 400 rationale — provider.DashboardModel (provider/dashboard.go:126-152) has no tools/JSON/vision flags. Confirmed. See finding 3 for the one caveat. Health — health.Status is provider-level (health.go:124: ProviderID, key-test OK/latency); Checker.Last()/History() are provider-keyed; no per-model probe exists anywhere in internal/health. Ring fields match the response 1:1 (HistorySummary: Samples, Uptime1h/24h as percent, P50Ms/P95Ms over successful probes — health.go:99-114). probe_scope:"provider" + the note are in the response body, not just a comment (fixture asserts them). No-checker → all unknown with nulls (tested). ?provider= unknown → empty 200 (probed). Route conflict — Go 1.22+ ServeMux prefers literals over wildcards regardless of registration order; TestM810LiteralRoutesBeatDetailWildcard proves it on the exact patterns proposed. s.register (guard.go:340) panics on unclassified routes and matches guard rows by exact template string, so the guard rows must use byte-identical paths. The scope test walks RouteTable() (guard_test.go:48,516,580), so the new rows are auto-covered once added. Fixtures — read all 7: every one "fixture": true, provider: "mock-upstream", recorded_from states mock/no-live-call, no invented prices (the 1.0 blended in the detail fixture is computed from the fixture registry's real 0.5/1.5 legs). PARITY row confirmed at docs/PARITY.md:1020: GET/POST /v1/models/query, /health, /[...model] | HAVE (3 extra files) | — | MISSING. Concurrency — handlers read lock-guarded or fresh state (registry List() clones under RLock, Checker.Last() locks, adapter builds a fresh slice). One pre-existing shared exposure noted in finding 5.
Findings: (1) Virtual models unreachable via the proposed detail route (fix at registration — no slice-code change). m810ListModels lists auto, auto/fast, auto/cheap, auto/coding, auto/code, auto/reasoning, intent/* (autorouter.go:214, intent.go:114) — all but auto contain /. Go's {model} matches a SINGLE segment, so GET /v1/models/auto/cheap never reaches the handler (mux-level 404, empty body) even though /v1/models advertises the model. Probed: /v1/models/a/b and /v1/models/a%2Fb → 404. No real catalog model ID contains / (grep clean), so this is confined to the synthetic virtual models — but they are real request targets the list advertises. FIX (coordinator): register GET /v1/models/{model...} (Go ... wildcard matches remaining segments) in BOTH server.go and guard.go with the identical string, and add a test GET /v1/models/auto/cheap → 200. Literal /v1/models/query and /v1/models/health still win over the ... wildcard. Blocking for registration only, not for the slice. (2) Query silently ignores ?category=, ?max_price=, ?min_context=, ?shortlist= (nit). Probed: GET /v1/models/query?category=free returns all 5 rows, unfiltered — the exact "silently returns everything" shape the capability 400 was introduced to avoid. Fix-or-document at merge: either route these through the shared parseModelFilters/filterV1Models or 400 unknown filter params; at minimum document that query supports only provider + free-tier-only. Not blocking (stated scope is provider + free-tier-only; /v1/models itself ignores unknown params by convention). (3) Capability-400 rationale is catalog-true but incomplete (nit). Capability data DOES exist in one narrow place: config.AutoRouter.CodeCapabilities map[string][]string (config.go:498-503), operator-declared per-model capabilities consumed for auto/code pool narrowing (main.go:1009-1010). It is sparse operator declaration, not measured catalog data, and cannot honestly honour a general ?capability=vision/tools/JSON filter — so the 400 stands and is the right call. But the error message and the file comment should name it ("the catalog records no flags; the only capability data is operator-declared CodeCapabilities for code-pool narrowing") so a future reader doesn't "fix" the 400 by wiring the wrong source. Also revisit if CodeCapabilities ever gains general use. (4) Query ordering differs from /v1/models (nit). handleModels sorts shortlisted rows first via filterV1Models; handleModelsQuery returns registry order (it marks Shortlisted via m810Detail but never sorts). Fix: stable-sort shortlisted-first in handleModelsQuery, or document the difference. (5) Pre-existing price-book write/read exposure (observation, not this slice). pricing_api.go:424 mutates s.priceBook.Pairs[key] at runtime (pricing override) while resolveModelPrice/rowBlendedPriceUSDPer1M read the same map without a lock — the same exposure all existing price readers share. No action for M8.10; flagging so the coordinator doesn't attribute it to this slice.
Registration snippet (verified, for coordinator merge): order does not matter to Go's mux (literals beat wildcards), but keep literals first for readability. Guard rows must be added before the s.register calls (register() panics on an unclassified route). Note the {model...} change from the builder's proposal (finding 1) — use the identical string in both places. guard.go — guardRouteTable (TierManagement, gateway-key auth, scope "models", same as GET /v1/models at guard.go:160): GET /v1/models/{model...} (single model detail — catalog entry for one model; unknown models answer 404); GET /v1/models/query (filter by provider and free-tier-only; capability filter answers 400 — catalog records no per-model capability flags); GET /v1/models/health (per-model health from the health-probe ring — each model's status is its provider's last probe result; unprobed models report unknown). server.go — register(): s.register GET /v1/models/query handleModelsQuery; GET /v1/models/health handleModelsHealth; GET /v1/models/{model...} handleModelDetail.
PARITY / scope agreement: agree MISSING → PARTIAL, not FULL. All three routes built and fixture-tested, but unregistered, unauthenticated-until-guard-wired, and with no live verification. Builder correctly did not touch PARITY.md; coordinator flips the row. Agree TierManagement + scope ["models"] for all three routes — identical to GET /v1/models (guard.go:160), and the table-driven scope test will cover the new rows automatically.
Limitations of this review: not registered or live-verified (same as the builder's limitation 1) — reviewer verified the test-only mux, not the production mux. Reviewer confirms the builder's limitations 2–4 as accurate: the two-seam base-URL logic (with omitted-not-false on resolver failure), the unreachable-in-practice free-category branch (no catalog entry uses CategoryFree — only the constant exists; the $0 price-book path is the live free signal and is tested), and provider-granular health.

Endpoint names — verified independently against OpenAI docs: fetched developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/speech-to-text (crawled 2026-11-19) and searched the Audio API reference: CONFIRMED POST /v1/audio/speech (JSON in, audio bytes out), POST /v1/audio/transcriptions (multipart in, JSON {"text"} out), POST /v1/audio/translations (multipart in, English JSON out) are real OpenAI endpoints; files up to 25 MB confirmed (guide: "Files can be up to 25 MB"). Passthrough of the existing endpoints is still correct; worth one line in PARITY/docs, not a code change.
Alias verdict: KEEP. Both aliases are one-line delegations to the shared handleAudio with the canonical op (audio.go: handleAudioTextToSpeech → AudioSpeech, handleAudioSpeechToText → AudioTranscriptions), so zero divergence risk — identical validation, headers, errors, and X-Codvian-Endpoint. PARITY row explicitly lists these OmniRoute names as the target, and the router's goal is OmniRoute parity, so serving them as documented aliases (code comments + fixtures both say "NOT an OpenAI endpoint") is the right call. Dead surface for pure OpenAI-SDK clients, but that is the point of parity with OmniRoute.
Dead-in-production gap — confirmed, and the builder's justification is WRONG: supportsAudio (audio_passthrough.go) requires the client to implement AudioPassthroughClient AND speak OpenAI wire; singleAudioAttempt skips non-capable targets pre-dial, and with none capable returns &NoCapableProviderError{Endpoint, Model} → handleAudioEngineError → honest 400 naming the endpoint (audio.go:504). NO provider client implements the interface — *openAIClient has only Chat/ChatStream/Embed/Rerank/ListModels/TestKey (+ PostImage/MediaPassthrough from sibling slices). This slice could have done the same with internal/provider/audio_client.go. MERGE GATE: do not flip PARITY to PARTIAL or present audio as working until the follow-up lands and one end-to-end call succeeds (mock upstream is enough to prove wiring; live provider proves the transport).
Follow-up contract (each point verified against the images precedent + client.go): audioURL(op) = joinAPIPath(c.baseURL, "audio/"+string(op)) — same pattern as imagesURL (images_client.go:209); audioUpstreamPath already returns audio/speech etc. Catalog auth via newRequest (client.go:1373) — applies catalog-resolved authHeaders, else Bearer apiKey; override Content-Type with the caller's value for multipart exactly as PostImage does (images_client.go:223). Non-2xx → *provider.UpstreamError via doForEndpoint → mapStatusErrorForEndpoint — but do NOT copy PostImage blindly: (a) it json.Unmarshals the body, which would fail on raw audio bytes — the audio client must return bytes untouched; (b) it ignores response headers and hardcodes status 200, while doForEndpoint returns (body, header, err) but NOT the status — the audio client needs the real status + Content-Type from resp.Header, so it needs a new transport helper (or a doForEndpoint-variant returning status); (c) doForEndpoint reads via io.LimitReader(resp.Body, maxResponseBody) (10 MiB, client.go:1371) and SILENTLY TRUNCATES — for images the truncation fails honestly as malformed JSON, but for audio bytes there is no parse step, so a >10 MiB TTS clip would be delivered as a CORRUPT TRUNCATED FILE with HTTP 200. The follow-up must detect truncation (e.g. compare bytes read against Content-Length) and fail honestly. The builder's "will truncate" framing understates this: it is silent data corruption, not truncation-with-error. Accept: application/json is set by newRequest — verify live whether OpenAI ignores it for speech or override it for the speech op. Native-wire clients will inherit the method via embedding *openAIClient (as with images); harmless because supportsAudio never selects them.
singleAudioAttempt mirror — verified faithful; drift risk is real: mechanical diff of singleAudioAttempt vs singleAttempt (embeddings.go:248): the ONLY differences are the signature (extra op, hardcoded supportsAudio instead of the supports param), the projection call (checkAudioEndpointStrategy vs checkEndpointStrategy), the skip comment wording, and a dropped panic-isolation COMMENT (the defer recover logic itself is present and identical). Chain walk, orderAttempts, breaker/cooldown filtering, budgetGate, limiter-shed continue, exactly-once return, noteSuccess/noteFailure, markExempt, recordDecision, trace publishing, and the sawCapable → NoCapableProviderError tail are all byte-identical in logic. Underlying claim also true: cascade.go:endpointForProjection has NO audio case — any audio kind falls to default: → "unknown endpoint kind %q" — and the default strategy "priority" IS a combo strategy (autovariants.go:1105, NonChatStrategies), so singleAttempt would reject every audio call. Concurrent M8.20 edit is real (git diff on cascade.go: adds search/OCR/video/music cases to that same switch, NOT audio). Mirror was the right call for this slice (cascade.go is forbidden to it, and the concurrent edit is live), but drift risk is genuine: COORDINATOR should fold the three audio cases into endpointForProjection when the M8.20 slice merges, then delete singleAudioAttempt in favour of singleAttempt. Related nit: audio EndpointKind constants are defined locally as strings in the router package while the provider taxonomy owns them — the media slice precedent (media_verify.go defines EndpointSearch/EndpointOCR/EndpointVideo/EndpointMusic in a new provider file) shows the cleaner home.
Fusion test verified: TestAudioChatOnlyStrategyFailsClosed sets Strategy: "fusion" and asserts the error names fusion and audio-speech with 0 upstream calls — it passes, and the reasoning is sound: fusion is a chat-only multi-model synthesis strategy meaningless for a single-shot audio call, so failing closed (via autorouter.ProjectStrategyForEndpoint on the known audio endpoints) instead of silently substituting priority is correct.
Byte-for-byte multipart — verified: handleAudio reads the body once (audio.go, io.ReadAll(r.Body)), scans it with extractAudioMultipart (multipart framing only — NextPart skips unread file bytes without copying; only the short model part is read), and forwards the SAME body slice plus the verbatim Content-Type (boundary included) to the engine, which passes the same slice to the client. No decode/rebuild path exists anywhere — the scan cannot diverge from what is forwarded because it operates on the forwarded bytes. Tests assert byte-equality and boundary preservation at both layers.
Verbatim response — verified, with the truncation caveat above: on success the server writes the provider's status (0→200 default), Content-Type iff non-empty, and body bytes untouched, plus only gateway headers (X-Codvian-Endpoint, decision/telemetry headers). No header stripping of anything the contract promises (only Content-Type is forwarded; other upstream headers are dropped by design, as with embeddings). Audio bytes are BUFFERED, not streamed (full clip in memory at transport + handler, same slice, no extra copy) — acceptable for a passthrough, and the 10 MiB response cap lives in doForEndpoint, not in this slice.
Findings: (1) Missing provider client — dead in production (follow-up, merge gate, not slice-blocking). internal/router/audio_passthrough.go, internal/provider/client.go (absence). No *openAIClient.AudioPassthrough exists. Fix: add internal/provider/audio_client.go per the contract above (incl. truncation detection and status/Content-Type preservation). Gate: PARITY stays MISSING (or PARTIAL only with the gap explicitly recorded) until it lands. (2) Guardrail gap is understated — transcriptions/translations DO carry text (nit, follow-up). internal/server/audio.go:extractAudioMultipart + package doc claim "an audio upload has no text to scan". OpenAI's own guide (fetched) documents an optional prompt field (plus keywords/languages) on transcriptions — free attacker-controlled text that reaches the upstream model unexamined; whisper-1 prompts have a 224-token limit. Fix: extract the prompt part in extractAudioMultipart, pass it as Text for the guardrail scan (and the bookkeeping estimate), and correct the doc comment. (3) Strict per-request budget gate fails OPEN for audio — verified, follow-up (nit). Three layers all admit unpriced audio: engine budgetGate returns (false, 0) when prompt count is 0 (uploads: no messages) or estimate is $0 (unpriced pair) (requestbudget.go:108); server enforceRequestBudget returns false when price.InputUSDPer1M == nil; enforcePromptBudget likewise. Matches the codebase's established unpriced policy (router.go:667, "unpriced requests proceed") and the embeddings token-ID precedent — but for audio it is TOTAL, not partial: audio never carries token usage, so a strict budget can NEVER refuse an audio call and key-budget reservations commit ~$0. Ledger side itself is honest (verified: ledger.go stores price_known=0 + cost 0 for split-unknown rows, and attemptCost returns 0 for local-estimate/zero usage — cost is "unknown", never a priced $0). Fix in the provider/pricing follow-up: per-minute/per-character audio pricing or a request-count budget axis. (4) Uncapped upload body — acceptable open limit only as a recorded risk; recommend capping now (nit, merge gate recommended). internal/server/audio.go: io.ReadAll(r.Body) with no MaxBytesReader, and the read happens BEFORE the s.timeout context is created, so the timeout does not bound the read either; there is NO ReadTimeout/WriteTimeout anywhere in cmd//internal/server//internal/config/, and no global body cap exists (every maxRequestBody use is per-handler; server.go:4019 is inside handleChatCompletions only). One 100 MB upload ≈ 100 MB RSS (single copy — handler + engine + transport share the slice); concurrent uploads multiply it. Builder's reason for no cap (10 MiB would reject legitimate 10–25 MB files) does not justify NO cap: 25 MiB is the upstream-derived cap, verified from OpenAI docs, not an invented number. Fix: io.ReadAll(http.MaxBytesReader(w, r.Body, 25<<20)) + 413, plus a test. Cheap; do it before registration. (5) callAudio's synthetic non-2xx error discards the upstream error body (nit). audio_passthrough.go:callAudio builds UpstreamError{Body: "audio upstream returned status %d"}. Only reachable if a transport returns non-2xx as success (the contract forbids it), so low impact. Fix: include the response body (truncated/redacted) when present. (6) singleAudioAttempt drift + local endpoint-kind strings (nit, noted above). Fix: fold audio cases into cascade.go:endpointForProjection at the M8.20 merge and delete the mirror; consider moving EndpointAudio* constants into the provider taxonomy via a new file, as the media slice did. (7) Observation, not a defect: TestAudioSpeechFixtureContract took 4.31 s vs <0.15 s for the other server tests (likely test-server/guardrail init). Passing; no action.
Registration snippet (constructed by reviewer, verified against the embeddings precedent): guard.go — insert after the rerank row (guard.go:163), same shape as embeddings/rerank (TierManagement, Scopes: ["chat"]): POST /v1/audio/speech (audio speech text-to-speech — OpenAI-compatible, routed through the same engine as chat completions but replay-exempt: exactly one upstream attempt, never replayed onto another target); POST /v1/audio/text-to-speech (alias of POST /v1/audio/speech — not an OpenAI endpoint; OmniRoute route name, same handler and operation); POST /v1/audio/transcriptions (speech-to-text — OpenAI-compatible multipart upload, replay-exempt); POST /v1/audio/speech-to-text (alias of transcriptions — not an OpenAI endpoint; OmniRoute route name); POST /v1/audio/translations (translate audio to English — OpenAI-compatible multipart upload, replay-exempt). server.go — insert after line 1325 (/v1/rerank registration). Order matters: register() PANICS if the guard row is missing (guard.go:340), so guard rows go in first: s.register POST /v1/audio/speech handleAudioSpeech; POST /v1/audio/text-to-speech handleAudioTextToSpeech; POST /v1/audio/transcriptions handleAudioTranscriptions; POST /v1/audio/speech-to-text handleAudioSpeechToText; POST /v1/audio/translations handleAudioTranslations. Also required at registration: add the 5 paths to the expected list in TestRouteTableSpawnCapableAreLocalOnly (guard_test.go:89) and update the scope-vocabulary comment (guard_test.go:584, currently lists chat/embeddings/rerank/messages/responses) to include audio. TestScopeEnforcedTableDriven (guard_test.go:608) and TestGuardTableScopesComplete (:779) auto-cover new rows via RouteTable() — run the full guard suite after registration to confirm 401/403 behaviour, since this slice's server tests invoke handlers directly and never exercise guard-table auth.
Agreement checks: PARITY MISSING → PARTIAL (not FULL): agree as the target, with the condition in finding 1 — sound-generation, /v1/audio/voices, and text-to-speech/[voiceId] are indeed unbuilt and the row (docs/PARITY.md:1027) lists exactly those. The row must record the no-provider-client gap until the follow-up lands. TierManagement + chat scope for all five: agree. Matches the embeddings/rerank precedent (guard.go:162–163) verbatim in structure; audio is a model-invoking inference call like them, no audio scope exists, and TestGuardTableScopesComplete requires every guarded route to declare a scope.
