Fixtures: read all 5 fully. All have "fixture": true, schema_version: 1, recorded_from mock notes, Bearer <redacted> placeholders (tests substitute their own key). No invented prices — rates are the tests' own mock price book ($10/$30 per 1M mock-model; cheap-model $0.5/$1.5 mock registry).
Claims verified true (with the code read): Session detail reuses meSessionRow verbatim — quotas.go embeds meSessionRow and calls s.meSessionRow(root, prices) (mesessions.go:92), the same function the list uses. Not a copy; no drift possible. Turn-id resolution + cycle guard — meSessionRoot (quotas.go) walks PreviousID to the root with a seen map and a 10,000-step cap; cycle, broken chain, or unknown id all return false → honest 404. meSessionTurns walks Children with its own seen set. No infinite loop path. 404s, never 403 — unknown id, other key's session, and keyless pre-M5.6 record all answer 404 (quotas.go ownership check is on the root, root.KeyID == "" || root.KeyID != keyID → 404). Deliberate information-hiding, consistent with the list endpoint, which filters rec.KeyID == keyID (mesessions.go). 401 without a key, 405 for non-GET — both probed by tests and passing. Turns pricing — turns are oldest-first (sort by CreatedAt, tie-break by ID), priced at CURRENT catalog prices, nil when unpriced, never $0. Totals use the same current-price formula (list price, not ledger cost) — the list endpoint's documented rule (mesessions.go header: "each turn repriced at the catalog's current list prices… the same rule OmniRoute documents"), so list and detail agree. Consequence worth noting, not a defect: the sum of turns_detail list prices equals the total except when truncated at 1000 turns — but that is disclosed by turns_detail_truncated: true, and totals are never capped. The 1000-turn cap + flag are real (meSessionDetailMaxTurns = 1000). Quota check calls the real gate, verbatim — ledger.CheckKeyBudget (keybudget.go:773) and ledger.CheckKeyTokenLimits (tokenlimit.go:71), the same functions the live path calls (keybudgets.go:137,174). 5h cap confirmed (RollingWindow = 5 * time.Hour, keybudget.go:46); active calendar window + unpriced fail-closed (incl. month-closed) confirmed in enforceKeyBudgetCaps / enforceKeyBudgetUnpriced. Check's budget projection mirrors enforceKeyBudgetCaps exactly: spent + estimate >= cap for the 5h cap and the active window (st.LimitUSD == b.LimitUSD, st.SpentUSD = 5h priced spend + reservations, keybudget.go:614-655). "Never more permissive than the gate" — TRUE for the token-limit axis, as the task framed it. CheckKeyTokenLimits breaches only when used >= limit (tokenlimit.go: if used < lim.Limit { continue }) — the live gate admits the over-pushing request and refuses the next. The check denies when used + estimate >= limit (WouldExceed, quotas.go). Proved from code both ways. Projection is ReserveSpend semantics for the key-budget store — ReserveSpend (reservation.go:57) refuses via the same enforceKeyBudgetCaps(st, b, estimateUSD). estimated_cost_basis is real and disclosed in the response — "split" vs "input_rate_only" is a JSON field, asserted by the allowed fixture (estimated_cost_usd: 0.001 = 100 tokens × $10/1M input rate). See Finding 3 for the caveat. Token-limit rows come from the ledger, never estimated — quotaCheckTokenUsed sums UsageSlices filtered by scope. UsageSlices (usageslices.go:41) is ok=1 AND shared=0 grouped by (provider, model); Rows is COUNT(*). The gate's tokenWindowUsed omits the shared filter, but the sums agree because shared waiter rows are zeroed at record time (normalizeUsage, ledger.go: shared rows get tokens/cost zeroed). Failed-row exclusion is proven by TestTotalRequestsInSlices (3 seeded rows → 3; after recording an OK: false row, still 3). total_requests definition + patch placement — sum of UsageSlices.Rows = successful non-shared rows, the same population the window's spend/tokens count (spend/tokens in usageWindow come from the same slices). Patch placement verified: field goes in meStatusUsageWindow after UnpricedRequests (keystatus.go:102), accumulation w.TotalRequests += sl.Rows goes inside usageWindow's for _, sl := range slices loop next to w.Tokens += sl.TotalTokens (keystatus.go:222), alongside the existing w.UnpricedRequests += sl.Rows precedent. No double-counting: usageWindow is called once per window (cost/daily/weekly) over that window's own slices. 200-on-refusal — allowed: false as HTTP 200 is a deliberate, documented design choice (a verdict, not a check failure). Note the nearest precedent cuts the other way (om-usage returns allowed: false WITH an error status, usageapi.go:421), so this is a new convention for a pre-flight endpoint, not an established one — acceptable, but it is a choice, not a precedent. Validation 400s name the field — "model is required", "estimated token counts must not be negative", "estimated_prompt_tokens + estimated_completion_tokens must equal estimated_tokens" (all in quotas.go, probed by TestQuotasCheckValidation, passing). No-ledger 503 is fail-closed and reachable only when a server is built without a ledger (production always wires one). Routing conflict: none. Go's ServeMux allows exact "/v1/me/sessions" and wildcard "/v1/me/sessions/{id}" side by side; the {id} shape has precedent in the guard table (/v1/responses/{id}, guard.go:166). Concurrency is safe: handlers read the mutex-guarded responses store (responses.go: Get/Children/Snapshot lock s.mu), sql.DB, and config lookups; no shared mutable state in the new code.
Findings: (1) Nit (highest priority — resolve before registration): legacy cumulative budget cap is never consulted, so the header's "never approves a request the machinery would refuse" is overstated. internal/server/quotas.go header + handleQuotasCheck consult only the rolling key_budgets store. But the live chat path ALSO enforces the legacy budgets-table cap: enforcePromptBudget (server.go:4150, called on chat, responses, audio, images, embeddings, completions) → ledger.CheckBudgetForEstimate (budget.go:131-200: "The two stores are the legacy per-key cap (SetBudget) and the rolling 5-hour key budget… Either store can refuse"), and ReserveSpend also refuses on the cumulative cap (reservation.go, M5.14) — both FailClosed-gated. A key with a legacy budgets row exhausted gets allowed: true from the check but 402 from a real chat request. FIX (either): in handleQuotasCheck, also consult the legacy store (e.g. ledger.CheckBudgetForEstimate(ctx, keyID, estCost) / GetBudget) and deny budget_exceeded when it refuses; OR narrow the header claim to the rolling key-budget store and document the exclusion next to the traffic-limits exclusion. Why a nit, not FAIL: no live server writer for the legacy store exists (SetBudget has no non-test server caller; the key-budgets API writes only key_budgets), rows arise only via import/older versions, it requires FailClosed on, and the endpoint is unregistered so nothing is live. But the claim as written is false for that axis, and the traffic exclusion WAS disclosed while this one was not. (2) Nit: auto/* virtual models are not resolved. The live gate resolves virtual models before the unpriced check (checkKeyBudgetForModel skips the unpriced check for virtual models; reserveKeyBudget uses budgetCheckModels). quotas.go passes the raw model to CheckKeyBudget, so model: "auto/..." under fail_closed yields unpriced_model — a false refusal vs the live gate. Safe direction (stricter, never more permissive). FIX: document that callers must pass concrete models, or resolve via the same helper the live path uses. (3) Nit: input_rate_only lower-bound is not disclosed in the response. The payload carries the basis string but nothing saying the figure is a lower bound (only code comments + fixture notes say so), and the bound holds only when output rate ≥ input rate — if a model prices output below input, total-at-input-rate is an UPPER bound. The code comment conditions it correctly ("a lower bound whenever output costs more than input"). FIX: note the bound in the coordinator's API docs (or a response note field). (4) Nit: provider is caller-asserted, never validated. An unknown/mismatched provider silently skips provider-scoped token limits and falls back to model-level pricing, so a wrong provider can yield allowed: true where the live request (real provider) would be refused on a provider-scoped limit. The caller fools only themselves — the live gate re-enforces — but FIX: document that provider is advisory/caller-asserted. (5) Nit: s.cfg == nil silently skips token limits. Step 3 of handleQuotasCheck is guarded by if s.cfg != nil, while the budget half fails closed on a nil ledger. Production always has a config. FIX: return 503 when s.cfg is nil and limits cannot be read, for symmetry. (6) Decision point (not a defect): chat scope is stricter than sibling me/* routes. /v1/me/status and /v1/me/sessions guard rows carry no Scopes (any gateway key), while the proposal requires chat. For session detail this mirrors /v1/responses/{id} (chat, guard.go:166) — agree. For quotas/check the rationale holds — /v1/chat/completions itself requires chat (guard.go:161), so the keys that send the requests being checked have it. Consequence to accept knowingly: models-only keys get 403 here while they can call /v1/me/status.

Gateway-key auth required." Scopes ["chat"]. ## M8.20 review — FAIL, one blocking defect (2026-11-19 ~00:40 IST)
Independent review verdict: FAIL (one blocking defect). Reviewer verified everything from scratch: read all 6 slice files and all 5 fixtures in full, re-ran every test, re-checked every citation against the live web, probed the catalog, and ran a toggle proof with a /tmp overlay (tree left byte-identical). Slice well-built and almost everything the re-dispatch claimed checks out — except one real defect the tests never exercise, which is blocking because its failure mode is silent corruption delivered as HTTP 200.
F1 — BLOCKING: MiniMax music responses are silently truncated above 10 MiB, delivering corrupt JSON as a 200. internal/provider/media_verify.go (~line 240): body, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, maxResponseBody)), where maxResponseBody is the shared 10 * 1024 * 1024 (internal/provider/client.go:1371). LimitReader truncates silently — no error — and the truncated bytes are returned verbatim with the upstream 200 status, then written out by serveMediaVerify as a successful response. Confirmed against MiniMax's own docs (fetched live 2026-11-19, platform.minimax.io/docs/api-reference/music-generation): output_format defaults to hex — the default response carries the whole song as hex-encoded audio. Docs' own example: 25,364 ms, music_size 813,651 bytes (~0.8 MB binary → ~1.6 MB hex). Scaling linearly, a 5-minute song at the default 256 kbps is ~9.6 MB binary → ~19 MB hex, nearly 2× the cap. Songs longer than ~2.7 min at 256 kbps are cut mid-JSON and handed to the client as a valid 200. Slice's fixture audio is "00ff" (4 chars), so no test can catch this. FIX: in MediaPassthrough, read with a media-appropriate cap (e.g. 64 MiB) AND detect truncation — if the body hits the cap and more data remains, return an honest error instead of corrupt bytes. A test with a >10 MiB canned upstream body (e.g. a realistic 4-minute hex payload) must be added; the current fixture-only coverage is what let this through.
Nits (non-blocking): (2) Unknown video task id answers 502, not an honest 404. internal/provider/media_verify.go: upstream 404 becomes *UpstreamError; classifyStatus (internal/provider/taxonomy.go) classifies 404 as DecisionFallback, and statusForEngineError (internal/server/telemetry.go:78) only preserves 4xx statuses on DecisionStop, so the gateway returns 502 for "no such task". Client cannot distinguish unknown-id from upstream-down. Fix: in serveMediaVerify's engine-error path (or handleEmbedEngineError), map a 404 UpstreamError to 404. Note GET does dial the upstream task endpoint each call — no stored record and no polling loop; the client polls by re-GETting. (3) 120 s gateway timeout vs synchronous MiniMax generation. DefaultTimeout = 120s (internal/server/server.go:87); MiniMax music is synchronous and takes "tens of seconds to several minutes" (third-party live-call reports corroborate the docs' synchronous shape). A >120 s generation times out as 504 after the paid job ($0.15, documented) has run upstream, and music has no task id to recover it with — exactly-once forbids a retry. Fix: document at wiring, or give the music handler a longer per-endpoint timeout. (4) OCR 10 MiB request cap is stricter than the upstream. internal/server/media_verify.go: body capped at 10 MiB "like chat". Mistral OCR accepts documents up to 50 MB / 1000 pages, so a base64 document between 10–50 MB is refused 400 by the gateway although the upstream would take it. Fix: note it, or raise the cap for /v1/ocr only. (URL documents, the common path, are unaffected.) (5) Plugin model rewrite is recorded but never reaches the upstream. internal/server/media_verify.go: if bookReq.Model != model { model = bookReq.Model } syncs the rewrite onto mediaReq.Model (routing/ledger label) only; the body travels verbatim, so the upstream still receives the original model while the ledger records the rewritten one. Fix: correct the comment ("synced onto the real request") or drop the sync for media. (6) No model allow-listing: any non-empty model passes. Handlers require model non-empty but never validate it against the pinned provider's models (e.g. music-3.0-free, discontinued 2026-08-20, or a nonsense string sails through to the upstream, which rejects it honestly). Deliberate verbatim design and the upstream fails honestly, so not a defect — but the music-3.0 pin the slice advertises is a convention, not an enforcement. (7) Note (not a defect) — "cost unknown" is gateway-honest but the prices are published. No price-book entries exist, so cost stays unknown in the ledger — correct. But the docs verified do publish prices: Perplexity Search $5.00/1,000 successful requests ($1.00 fast), MiniMax music $0.15/generation. Price-book follow-up belongs to the coordinator, not this slice. (8) Note — ReplayExemption (internal/router/preread.go:109) does not list the media endpoints. Harmless in practice: singleAttempt (internal/router/embeddings.go:248) returns after the first called attempt regardless of the exemption list, and markExempt stamps the trace exempt:<endpoint> — the router test asserts exempt:search and passes. The switch is now an incomplete codification of the exemption list; consider adding the four kinds for consistency.
Verified as correct (spot-check summary): Provider pinning / exactly-once — chainForProvider builds the chain from keys of req.ProviderID only (router/media_verify.go:59); singleAttempt returns after the first called attempt, success or failure; pre-dial skips (cooldown/breaker/unsupported/limiter-shed) fall through. Confirmed by code + tests (second key never dialled after a 429). Missing pinned key: honest typed NoCapableProviderError → 400, no upstream-failure counter. Confirmed. Verbatim relay + raw path join: joinRawPath inserts no /v1; probed URLs are exactly https://api.perplexity.ai/search, https://api.mistral.ai/v1/ocr, https://api.dev.runwayml.com/v1/text_to_video, https://api.dev.runwayml.com/v1/tasks/{id}, https://api.minimax.io/v1/music_generation (provider tests assert the dialled paths against httptest). Auth/Content-Type un-overridable: newRequest sets them before ExtraHeaders are applied, and ExtraHeaders skips authorization/content-type/accept (provider test with planted headers passes). Music 200-envelope: returned verbatim, including a failure envelope (base_resp.status_code != 0 still answers HTTP 200) — honest per the docs, and the code says so. No polling exists for music (correct — MiniMax is synchronous). Search validation: 1–5 queries enforced; 0 and 6 both 400 (test TestMediaValidation covers 6). OCR accepts URL/base64/file-id documents as an object; no document size cap beyond the 10 MiB body cap (see nit 4). Handlers are stateless (package-level mediaSpec values are read-only). Cascade diff: git diff internal/router/cascade.go is exactly the 4-case endpointForProjection mapping (search/ocr/video/music → autorouter.EndpointSearch/OCR/Videos/Music) plus the comment — nothing else. internal/server/internalbudgets.go diff is the M8.19 notifyBudgetWarning line, not this slice. docs/LOG.md is coordinator/sibling work. No forbidden file touched by this slice; the 6 M8.20 files are untracked, nothing committed, no remote added. Unwired state confirmed: grep of guard.go/server.go finds zero references to handleSearch/handleOCR/handleVideo/handleMusic or the five paths — live serving is still 404, as honestly stated. Fixtures: all 5 labelled mock-upstream canned data (recorded_from: "mock-upstream… NOT a live provider call"), no invented prices/models/caps. No live provider call was made by the reviewer either. Re-dispatch "changed nothing": cannot be diffed against the first attempt's files (no baseline exists), but the current files are complete, tests pass unmodified, and git status shows no stray files beyond known sibling-slice churn. The re-dispatch's two SIGTERM exec failures left no trace in the tree; their cause is unverifiable from here. One transient environment flake of the reviewer's own: an early go build ./... printed "internal/degrade: open /tmp/go-build…/_pkg_.a: no such file or directory" (likely a sibling build racing the shared cache); immediate re-run exited 0.
Reviewer test runs (all re-run): go build ./... — exit 0; go vet ./internal/provider/ ./internal/router/ ./internal/server/ — exit 0; gofmt -l on all 6 M8.20 files + cascade.go — clean. Provider go test ./internal/provider/ -run 'TestMediaPassthrough|TestJoinRawPath' — 7/7 PASS. Router go test ./internal/router/ -run 'TestMediaPassthrough' — 7/7 PASS (verbatim, exactly-once, skipped-key fallthrough, typed no-provider errors, GET status preservation, validation). Server go test ./internal/server/ -run 'TestMedia' — 14/14 PASS (auth, validation, 5 happy paths, 503, engine-error mapping, 5 fixture contract tests). The mapping is load-bearing. ## M8.13 review — FAIL, one blocking defect, small fix (2026-11-19 ~00:38 IST)
Independent review verdict: FAIL — one blocking defect: the slice's headline claim "Errors are Gemini-shaped" is false for the entire pre-engine error path. Every 400 validation rejection (including all 26 unmapped-field cases the slice advertises), the 404 unknown-action, the 405s, the 503 engine-not-configured, and the response-conversion 502 come back in the OpenAI envelope {"error":{"message":…,"type":"invalid_request_error"}}, not the Gemini envelope {"error":{"code":…,"message":…,"status":…}}. Only post-translation engine errors use the Gemini envelope. A Gemini client gets two different error shapes on one surface, split at an arbitrary internal boundary. Proved empirically by the reviewer. Everything else checked verified — translation, wire, streaming, list, mux, scopes, and the toggle proofs all hold — so this is a one-defect FAIL with a precise, ~20-line fix.
Reviewer runs (all live-tree, no overlay needed except the probe): go test -count=1 -run 'TestM813' ./internal/server/ — PASS, 17/17 top-level (26 unmapped subtests incl. 1 control, 7 error-mapping subtests), 0.596s; go vet ./internal/server/ exit 0, no findings; go test -count=1 ./internal/apitest/ ok, 0.193s; overlay probe TestM813ReviewerProbe (-overlay from /tmp/m813probe_test.go, live tree untouched — git status clean of reviewer's files afterwards) — PASS (6 handler-level 400s, 7 boundary cases, ANY-arity, empty-role, GET-single-model). Env: /tmp 16% full now (builder's 82–90% caveat no longer applies); GOCACHE/TMPDIR under ~/workspace used as instructed. Reviewer's runs all succeeded live — no evidence the builder's failed exec calls were slice-caused; they look like sibling churn / service restarts. No geminiTestRegistry collision exists now (single declaration; the M8.14 note's stale caveat is moot).
Toggle proofs — reproduced: tampered status 201 vs 200 fails — TestM813FixtureToggleNegative passes in the reviewer's suite run (asserts CheckResponse errors). Models list empties with no Gemini models — covered by TestM813ModelsHandler (empty-list case), passes. Wire proof: TestM813GenerateContentFixture + TestM813StreamGenerateContentFixture pass; mock upstream saw POST /models/mock-gemini-model:generateContent (translated contents + generationConfig temperature 0.5 / maxOutputTokens 64) and :streamGenerateContent?alt=sse. No-Authorization verified in code, not just the test: newRequest (internal/provider/client.go:1382-1390) sends exactly authHeaders when non-nil, no Bearer default; test passes only x-goog-api-key. Catalog ResolveAuth for Gemini is x-goog-api-key (internal/catalog/catalog.go:546, verified.go:108).
F1 — BLOCKING: pre-engine errors are OpenAI-shaped, not Gemini-shaped. Files/lines: internal/server/gemini_native.go:937 (list 405), :977 (action 405), :992 (unknown action 404), :1016 (empty model 400), :1066 (413), :1068 (invalid body 400), :1074 (parse 400), :1079 (translation 400 — i.e. EVERY unmapped-field rejection), :1089 (writeServerError, 503), :1122 (writeUpstreamFailure, 502). Pre-call gate refusals and guardrail blocks (:1085, :1116 via applyGuardrails/applyResponseGuardrails → writePolicyRefusal, JSON-RPC code -32001) are also non-Gemini-shaped. Proof: writeError is documented "writes an OpenAI-shaped client error" (internal/server/server.go:1601-1603). Reviewer's overlay probe captured the live body for a topK 400: {"error":{"message":"generationConfig.topK is not supported on this gateway: top-K sampling has no mapping to the canonical request","type":"invalid_request_error"}} — no code, no status. Slice's own tests assert only status + message substring, never the envelope shape, so this shipped silently. Note the house precedent does NOT excuse it: /v1/responses uses writeError for 400s because it IS an OpenAI-native API; this surface's native shape is Gemini's, and the slice itself chose Gemini-shaped errors for engine errors (writeGeminiError, :1105, :1113), so the split is incoherent, not a design decision. FIX (in gemini_native.go only): add writeGeminiClientError(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, msg string) that writes newGeminiErrorEnvelope(status, geminiStatusForHTTP(status), msg); replace all writeError/writeServerError calls listed above with it; route writeUpstreamFailure at :1122 through writeGeminiError(errNilUpstreamResponse); and either route guardrail/gate blocks through the Gemini envelope too or record an explicit accepted exception. Add one test asserting a topK 400 body parses as geminiErrorEnvelope with code 400, status "INVALID_ARGUMENT".
F2 — Nit: test mux does not exactly mirror production auth. internal/server/gemini_native_test.go (newGeminiTestMux) wraps with requireAuth; production TierManagement registration uses requireAuthUnlessRelaxed (internal/server/guard.go:340+). In relaxed-auth mode the real route serves unauthenticated requests while the test proves a 401. Fix: note the difference in the test comment, or add a relaxed-mode case.
F3 — Nit: EndpointKind limitation wording is imprecise (substance correct). Builder says a distinct kind "would need a taxonomy.go change". Actually a constant could be declared in gemini_native.go (EndpointKind is a plain string type), but endpointForProjection (internal/router/cascade.go:278-295) FAIL-CLOSES on unknown kinds, so naming the surface distinctly needs a cascade.go case as well. Behaviour itself verified correct: unset Endpoint maps to the chat rule ("" → chat) and ReplayExemption (internal/router/preread.go:110) gives no exemption, so a Gemini request replays exactly like a chat completion — which is right (no upstream-held state). Fix: correct the comment; no code change needed.
F4 — Nit: single-model GET is missing; PARITY should be PARTIAL, not FULL. GET /v1beta/models/{model} (Gemini models.get) has no route: reviewer's mux probe and overlay probe both return 405 Method Not Allowed. Also no pagination (pageSize/pageToken, full list, no nextPageToken) — disclosed by the builder, fine as a known limitation.
F5 — Nit: POST /v1beta/models (bare) 307-redirects before 404ing. Mux probe: POST /v1beta/models → 307 to /v1beta/models/, which then hits rest="" → 404 unknown action "". Honest outcome, slightly odd path. No fix required; note it in the slice report.
F6 — Nit: "26 unmapped-field subtests" is 25 rejections + 1 control. The badArgsJSON case is a positive control (valid object args must translate). Count is right, label is slightly off. Also promptFeedback omitted (block reason unknown): the reason IS known at the wire layer (ConvertGeminiResponse, internal/wire/gemini.go:897, maps it to content_filter and drops it) but genuinely unknown by the time the handler converts, so the omission is honest — reword to "the canonical response carries no block reason".
F7 — Nit: client-supplied thinkingBudget can be overwritten by a pinned effort. Verified withEffortForwarding (internal/router/effort_forwarding.go:111) leaves req.ThinkingBudget untouched when ReasoningEffort == "", and the Gemini client sends it as thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget (internal/provider/gemini_client.go:122-129). But if a tier/group pins a named effort AND the operator table has a Gemini budget, the engine overwrites the client's value. Document that caveat.
F8 — Observation (no action): list includes keyless providers; prices genuinely unrecorded. Filter is exactly WireFormat == "gemini" (gemini_native.go, handleGeminiModels), and DashboardModels() (internal/provider/dashboard.go:157) lists all catalog providers regardless of key state — consistent with GET /v1/models; a listed model with no key fails at call time. DashboardModel carries price/context-window fields, but no static catalog source populates them for Gemini models (verified: no Gemini model entries with prices/windows in internal/catalog/*; prices/windows arrive only via the opt-in models.dev sync), so omitting them is honest, not a gap. Vertex exclusion verified honest: Vertex models are addressed as publishers/google/models/... (internal/provider/vertex_client.go:586-611), distinct wire format "vertex" (internal/provider/provider.go:65), so listing them under models/<id> would invent an address.
Verified claims (spot-checks, all confirmed in code + tests): Field mappings — role ""→user (probe: 200, role user); function turns → one tool message per part, ToolCallID = function name; inlineData → image_url data-URL → wire back to inlineData (internal/wire/gemini.go:813-836); thought parts → reasoning.State (SourceWire gemini), never content; toolConfig ANY 1 name → named function choice, >1 → {"type":"any"} (probe); thinkingBudget rides through (see finding 7). Unmapped → 400 naming the field (handler-level probe): topK, responseMimeType, safetySettings, cachedContent, fileData, googleSearch (via DisallowUnknownFields, error names the field) — all 400 with the field named. Boundaries (probe): temperature 2.0 ok / 2.0001 and −0.1 → 400; topP 1 ok / 1.1 → 400; 5 stops ok / 6 → 400. These match the real Gemini limits (temperature 0–2, topP 0–1, ≤5 stops). Response conversion: finish mapping stop/length/tool_calls/content_filter → STOP/MAX_TOKENS/TOOL_CALLS/SAFETY; empty/unknown omitted — FinishReason has omitempty (internal/wire/gemini.go:163), never invented. Usage total derived from legs only when 0 — mirrors TranslateGeminiStream exactly, no double-count. modelVersion = engine-reported model, falling back to the requested model (gemini_client.go: ChatStreamChunks sets res.Model = req.Model only when empty), so it is the model that actually served when upstream reports one. promptFeedback omitted — honest per finding 6. Streaming: geminiClient.ChatStreamChunks exists (internal/provider/gemini_client.go:202), upstream URL is :streamGenerateContent?alt=sse (:63). SSE shape verified against wire.TranslateGeminiStream (internal/wire/gemini.go:1019+): no [DONE] sentinel (stream simply ends; a literal [DONE] is tolerated defensively), terminal condition is the stream ending / finish reason; handler emits one data: event per text delta + a single terminal event with finish reason + usage + completed tool calls, no text repeat. Chunk-aware path drops tool-call deltas and re-emits completed calls in the terminal event (documented in code, correct). Error mapping (code + 7 subtests): 429 → RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED with Retry-After preserved; 401 → UNAUTHENTICATED (correct per Google semantics — the task's "PERMISSION_DENIED on 401" phrasing is wrong; 403 → PERMISSION_DENIED is right); QuotaError → PERMISSION_DENIED; upstream detail never exposed (all messages gateway-generated; UpstreamError body dropped). Temperature:0/topP:0 limitation: genuinely pre-existing and identical on /v1/chat/completions — Temperature/TopP are plain float64 (internal/provider/client.go:593-594, comment at :154: "0 means omit, use provider default"). MCP-loop absence: true — the Gemini client owns tool execution; tool calls are returned, never auto-executed (verified in gemini_client.go Chat path: tool calls are translated back, no execution loop). Consistent with /v1/messages handling of Gemini tool calls. Concurrency: handlers stateless; geminiNativeMethods is copied per row (append([]string(nil), …)). No shared mutable state. Standing rules: only the 3 claimed new files + 3 fixtures exist for this slice (internal/server/gemini_native.go, gemini_native_test.go, tests/fixtures/v1/m8.13/*.json, all "fixture": true, mock data only, no prices invented). No forbidden file touched by this slice — docs/LOG.md and internal/router/cascade.go show as modified, but those diffs are sibling slices' work (M8.14 review entries, etc.), not this slice. No remote added, nothing pushed/committed by the reviewer.
Registration snippet (for coordinator merge): guard table (internal/server/guard.go, next to line 160–161, same tier/scopes as the /v1/models / /v1/chat/completions precedents): GET /v1beta/models TierManagement Scopes ["models"] ("Gemini-native model list — gateway-key auth required."); POST /v1beta/models/{rest...} TierManagement Scopes ["chat"] ("Gemini-native generateContent / streamGenerateContent — gateway-key auth required."). Register calls (internal/server/server.go, next to lines 1322–1323; register() panics at startup if the guard row is missing, so add rows first): s.register(mux, "GET", "/v1beta/models", s.handleGeminiModels); s.register(mux, "POST", "/v1beta/models/{rest...}", s.handleGeminiModelAction). Mux-panic analysis (verified empirically with a standalone probe in /tmp/m813mux): POST /v1beta/models/{model}:generateContent panics at registration — parsing "POST /v1beta/models/{model}:generateContent": at offset 20: bad wildcard segment (must end with '}'). The two-route registration is complete for the three claimed operations (list + generate + stream); it is NOT complete for the full Gemini models resource (no GET /v1beta/models/{model} — returns 405 — and no pagination).

## M8.17 review — PASS-WITH-NITS (2026-11-19 ~00:36 IST)
Independent review verdict: PASS-WITH-NITS. No blocking defect. Slice does what it claims. Two honesty nits (one proven live by the reviewer's own probe) should be fixed at merge or recorded as known limitations; neither blocks the merge given both are disclosed, but finding 1 makes cost_status: "complete" literally false in one case, so the coordinator should decide explicitly.
Reviewer runs (all in a scratch copy at /tmp/m8-17-review, built with tar --exclude=dist --exclude=.git; only change vs live tree was deleting the sibling's broken internal/server/webhooks_api_test.go in the scratch; live tree left untouched — final git status confirms): gofmt -l on both slice files clean. go build ./internal/server/ clean in scratch AND in the live tree (PIPESTATUS 0). go vet ./internal/server/ exit 0, no findings. go test -vet=off -run 'TestAnalytics' -count=1 -v ./internal/server/ — 17/17 PASS (run twice, in two separately built scratch copies — /tmp was wiped twice by service restarts mid-review, so the copy was rebuilt from the live tree each time). go test -vet=off -race -run 'TestAnalytics' -count=1 — ok, exit 0, no data races. Exact values confirmed by the suite: seeded $0.04 priced row + unpriced row → requests 2, tokens 1500/1500/3000, priced_only, unpriced 1 req / 1000 tokens, mean latency 15 ms; complete case, failed-row-excluded, week/month boundaries, 405, 503.
Toggle proof re-run by reviewer (scratch-only probe file, 6 tests, all PASS): positive control (real handler + real fixture passes); wrong-status 201 fails; empty-object handler fails; tampered priced_cost_usd 0.05 fails against real 0.04 (fixture pins values, not just status); empty-window note present in raw JSON.
Honesty-critical claims — all confirmed in code: Summary sums cost_usd only (ledger.go:1618); unpriced rows record cost 0 and price_known=0 separately (RecordUsage, normalizeUsage at ledger.go:1132), so free-$0 vs unknown really are indistinguishable in Summary — the UsageSlices split is genuinely necessary and correctly used. Windows really are the ledger's calendar windows: parseAnalyticsWindow calls ledger.BudgetWindowFor with "00:00"; day = today 00:00→tomorrow, week = Monday→Monday, month = 1st→1st, all UTC (budgetwindow.go:85). Empty window is honest: has_data:false, cost_status:"no_data", note "no usage rows in this window" (verified in raw JSON by the reviewer's probe). No invented prices/models/caps: only mock provider mock-upstream, mock models; the $10/$30-per-1M price book exists only in the test file. All 7 fixtures carry "fixture": true and mock recorded_from. No total_cost field anywhere. No handler is reachable from the chat-completions path (handlers referenced only in analytics_api.go + its test). No div-by-zero / overflow: mean latency is SQL AVG(latency_ms) with COALESCE(...,0) — no Go division; token totals are SQL SUMs into int64. Handlers are stateless (per-request locals + thread-safe sql.DB + read-only config iteration); -race clean. No-GET → 405 on all four endpoints; no ledger → 503 on all four; unknown ?window= → 400 on all four (verified by suite + code).
Findings: (1) Nit (honesty — the important one): cost_status: "complete" can be false, with no note. analytics_api.go:127-141 (analyticsSplitFor iterates only s.cfg.GatewayKeys). Reviewer's probe seeded 1 priced row under the live key + 1 unpriced row under a key not in config; response: requests=2, priced_cost_usd=0.04, cost_status="complete", price_split_scope="gateway_keys_in_config", note="". The hole is real and the only in-response disclosure is the terse scope string — a consumer reading complete + empty note has no signal that deleted-key rows are excluded. Fix (coordinator's choice): add a ledger count of in-window rows whose gateway_key_id is not in config and surface it (e.g. out_of_scope_requests) with cost_status downgraded when > 0; or at minimum emit a note on the complete case stating the split covers keys still in config only. Disclosed in code comments and the brief, so nit, not blocking — but do not call the status unconditionally true. (2) Nit: unpriced_requests / unpriced_tokens are upper bounds, not exact counts. UsageSlices uses MIN(price_known) per (provider, model) group (usageslices.go:41), so one unpriced row taints the whole group. Reviewer's probe (1 priced + 1 unpriced row, same provider/model, same key) returned unpriced_requests=2, unpriced_tokens=3000 vs the true 1/1000. cost_status direction stays correct (priced_only), but the comment at analytics_api.go:123-126 ("counts successful requests whose price was unknown") is inaccurate in the mixed case (price added/removed mid-window). Fix: document as an upper bound, or compute the split per row (SUM(CASE WHEN price_known=0 ...)) via a new ledger query. (3) Nit: the empty-window note is asserted by no test in the slice. Neither empty fixture asserts note in json_paths, and no value test checks it (the priced_only note IS asserted in TestAnalyticsSummaryValues). Fix: add "note": "no usage rows in this window" to the two empty fixtures' json_paths. (4) Nit: 503 precedes 400. No-ledger is checked before window parsing, so bad ?window= + no ledger → 503, not 400. Minor ordering inconsistency; either order is defensible. (5) Observation, not a slice defect: the builder's "git diff --stat empty" is false in this live tree — but none of it is M8.17's. Tracked modifications present are other slices' concurrent work (internal/server/internalbudgets.go — M8.19 webhook call; internal/router/cascade.go; docs/LOG.md — coordinator's M8.14 review entry, added during the review). M8.17 itself touched no tracked file: its 2 source files + 7 fixtures are untracked new files, and all forbidden files (guard.go, server.go, config.go, docs/PARITY.md, docs/PLAN.md, docs/CHECKLIST.md, README.md, README.v2.md, web/index.html) are untouched by it. (Note: docs/LOG.md WAS clean at the reviewer's first check and got the M8.14 entry from another agent while the reviewer worked — shared-tree churn, not this slice.) (6) Environment caveat: the sibling compile blocker is real — the package test binary cannot compile in the live tree until internal/server/webhooks_api_test.go (undefined: io, buf) is fixed; the reviewer did not touch it. Also, one suite run transiently failed to build when the 512M /tmp tmpfs filled during the -race build (86% used); the rerun passed. The coordinator should re-run in the live tree once the sibling file is fixed, with /tmp headroom.
Registration snippet (verified, for coordinator merge): guard.go, next to the /api/usage/budget rows (lines 155–156), Tier 3 section: GET /api/analytics/summary, GET /api/analytics/by-provider, GET /api/analytics/by-model, GET /api/analytics/costs — all TierManagement, Scopes ["admin"], descriptions in the review handoff (read-only over stored ledger rows for one calendar window ?window=day|week|month, default day; summary: request/token/latency totals, priced spend + cost_status; by-provider/by-model: roll-ups with priced cost and unpriced-request count; costs: priced-spend + unpriced remainder, deliberately no total-cost field; all aggregate spend across all gateway keys — operator-only). server.go, next to lines 1317–1318: s.register lines for handleAnalyticsSummary / handleAnalyticsByProvider / handleAnalyticsByModel / handleAnalyticsCosts. Exact precedent is GET /api/usage/budget (guard.go:155: TierManagement, Scopes: ["admin"]); the spend-aggregating, operator-only rationale holds, and read-only means Tier 2 (destructive) does not apply. The 502 now goes through new writeGeminiUpstreamFailure, which preserves the old IncUpstreamFailure metrics counting and then calls writeGeminiError(errNilUpstreamResponse) → 502 / UNAVAILABLE / "upstream provider error", no upstream detail. - F1 — FIXED (blocking). MediaPassthrough now reads with a media-local cap mediaMaxResponseBody = 64 MiB (new const in internal/provider/media_verify.go) via LimitReader(cap+1), and returns an honest error ("media upstream response exceeds 67108864 bytes; refusing to return a truncated response") when the body hits the cap instead of returning corrupt JSON as a 200. The shared maxResponseBody (10 MiB, client.go) was NOT touched. New tests, payloads generated in memory (nothing written to /tmp or disk): TestMediaPassthroughLargeHexBodyUnderCapPassesIntact — a realistic 4-minute MiniMax hex payload (~15.36 MB hex, over the old 10 MiB cap) passes through byte-for-byte intact; TestMediaPassthroughOversizeBodyErrorsHonestly — a ~70 MB hex body errors naming the truncation.

Other errors still flow through handleEmbedEngineError unchanged. Comment records that GET dials the upstream task endpoint on every call (no stored record/polling loop; the client polls by re-GETting). New test TestMediaVideoTaskUnknownID404 pins 404 for an unknown video task id.

Comment on mediaMusicSpec + at the context.WithTimeout(r.Context(), s.timeout) site in internal/server/media_verify.go: synchronous MiniMax generation takes tens of seconds to several minutes vs the 120 s DefaultTimeout; a >120 s generation is cut off as 504 after the paid ($0.15) job has run, with no task id to recover it and exactly-once forbidding a retry. No longer timeout was set — no documented upper bound exists to ground a value, and a per-endpoint override needs shared timeout machinery outside this slice. Coordinator follow-up: plumb a music-specific (or per-endpoint) timeout through Server (shared server.go) — or an async task model — and record the chosen bound with its source.
- F4 — FIXED (cap raised, not just noted). New per-spec maxRequestBody field on mediaSpec; /v1/ocr is now 50 MiB (Mistral OCR accepts documents up to 50 MB / 1000 pages), all other surfaces keep the 10 MiB default. Comment notes URL documents (the common path) are unaffected. New test TestMediaOCRRequestBodyCap: an ~11 MB OCR body reaches the engine (200), a ~51 MB body is refused 400 before any dial.
- F5 — FIXED (comment corrected, sync kept). The plugin-rewrite comment now says the rewrite is recorded on the routing/ledger label only and the upstream body still carries the original model verbatim; the old "synced onto the real request" wording is gone. Chose comment correction over dropping the sync: dropping it would discard the plugin's rewrite from routing/ledger entirely (ledger would name a model the plugin explicitly replaced), while keeping it preserves the plugin's intent on the label and the verbatim-body rule keeps the upstream honest — the split is now stated, not hidden.

F7 — "cost unknown" stays; published prices (Perplexity Search $5.00/1,000, MiniMax $0.15/generation) are price-book follow-up for the coordinator. (The 3 tracked-file modifications in git status — docs/LOG.md, internal/router/cascade.go, internal/server/internalbudgets.go — pre-date this work; not touched by this fix.)
Verdicts:
- F1 — FIXED (blocking race). handleAPIPricingPatch in internal/server/pricing_api.go now acquires pricingMu BEFORE the validation loop (503 check on s.cfg/configPersist stays before the lock, as instructed). Validation, snapshots, apply, and configPersist all run under the same critical section, so PATCH-vs-PATCH and PATCH-vs-GET can no longer interleave on the book maps. The file-header CONCURRENCY claim and the pricingMu comment were corrected: they now state the lock covers GET snapshots AND PATCH validation + applies, and that handlePrices (dashboard feed, outside this slice) still reads the book unlocked — full race-freedom still needs a server-level mutex. One deliberate behaviour change: a PATCH that is both invalid AND has no persist wired now answers 503 (persist check first) instead of 400; all existing tests still pass.
- F3 — FIXED. cache_mgmt.go notes are now generic: semantic — "semantic cache not flushed: the wired semantic store does not expose FlushAll"; reasoning — "reasoning cache not flushed: the serving engine does not expose a reasoning flush". No internal/…, .go, or type names in any response note. The detail stays in the file header. New test TestM815CacheDeleteNotesAreGeneric pins this (fake SemanticStore with no FlushAll, entries_before 1, flushed false).
- F4 — FIXED (rule chosen + pinned). List mode is now documented in apiPricingTable as the ledger-effective table on both paths, with one rule: a ?provider= filter keeps pair rows for that provider AND the model-level fallback rows (they apply to that provider); the ledger path additionally lists book pair/synced/model entries the ledger doesn't cover — half-known pairs shown priced:false with the known side, exactly as single-pair lookup shows them. Book-only path: model rows are now included under a filter too (previously dropped). New test TestM815PricingGetListProviderFilter pins both paths, including that ?provider=other-provider keeps the fallback row but drops the mock pairs.
- F5 — FIXED (fix, not documentation). pricingPairKnown now treats LookupModel(model) + a known provider as pair-known (checked after registry/book/ledger have all had a chance to establish providerKnown; the fallback alone never makes the provider known). New test TestM815PricingPatchModelLevelFallback: book-only server, registry offers only mock-model, book holds a model-level fallback for shared-model → PATCH mock-upstream/shared-model now returns 200 (was 400) and GET shows 3/6. Note found while testing: the unknown-provider negative check must run BEFORE that PATCH — once the PATCH creates a ledger model-level entry, ledger.Prices.Known reports the pair priced for ANY provider, so a later PATCH for another provider is (consistently with GET) allowed. The test documents this ordering.
- F6 — FIXED. tests/fixtures/v1/m8.15/pricing-patch-400-invalid-rate.json: recorded_response.status 200 → 400 (now matches assert.status: 400). Key order in the file was normalised by the JSON rewrite; content otherwise unchanged.
- F2 — not code (report-count correction for the coordinator): confirmed the pre-fix suite was 17 top-level TestM815* tests, not 18.
- F7 — FIXED. New m815FlushStoreEngine (fake engine implementing cache.FlushStore, FlushAll returns 7) + TestM815CacheDeleteExactFlushStoreEngine: DELETE reports exact evicted 7, flushed true, FlushAll called exactly once, and the seeded exact cache still holds its 2 entries — proving DELETE routes through the engine, not the exactCacheFlushStore adapter (which would have reported 2). Total evicted 8 (7+1+0).
Proof: race probe (F1) TestM815PricingPatchConcurrentRace — 16 concurrent PATCHes to the same pair, run standalone under -race: PASS, zero DATA RACE warnings. Full suite under -race: 22/22 top-level PASS, 0 DATA RACE, exit 0. Full suite, plain -count=1: 22 top-level PASS (17 original + 5 new: PricingPatchConcurrentRace, PricingGetListProviderFilter, PricingPatchModelLevelFallback, CacheDeleteExactFlushStoreEngine, CacheDeleteNotesAreGeneric), plus 4 subtests under PricingPatchUnknownIDs — all PASS. go build ./internal/server/: OK. go vet ./internal/server/: OK. gofmt -l on all 4 Go files: clean (no output).
Deliberately left unchanged: handlePrices still reads the price book without a lock (outside this slice's owned files; header now says so honestly). The semantic-persistent adapter still offers no FlushAll and no shipped engine offers a reasoning flush — both still report honest flushed:false + generic notes; closing those gaps needs methods in files outside this slice. No behaviour change to the 503 path, rollback logic, or the saves counter (already serialised under the lock).

(The models-scope alternative for the read-only GETs, cf. auto-combo candidates guard.go:170, is a coordinator judgment call, not a defect.)
PARITY — builder's claims corrected (read-only study of ~/workspace/omniroute-reference):
Finding 1 — BLOCKING (for the PARITY edit, not the code): /v1/completions MISSING → FULL is not justified; correct is PARTIAL. OmniRoute src/app/api/v1/completions/route.ts + textCompletionTransform.ts: accepts BOTH messages[] and prompt bodies, JOINS multi-prompt arrays with "\n", STREAMS SSE translated to text_completion chunks, echoes the REQUESTED model in the body, maps ALL choices, and passes through logprobs + system_fingerprint. Ours: messages[] body → 400 "prompt is required", multi-prompt → 400, stream:true → 400, always exactly 1 choice, no system_fingerprint. The implemented subset (non-streaming, prompt-string, single completion) is real and well-tested, but it is a subset. Row should move to PARTIAL with the gaps listed.
Finding 2 — BLOCKING (for the PARITY edit): the lumped row POST /v1/classify, /v1/explain/routing, /v1/segment must be split — classify/segment as OmniRoute defines them are not implemented. Verified against source: /v1/classify is POST, a Jina zero/few-shot classification proxy (classify/route.ts, body {model?, classifier_id?, input, labels?} → api.jina.ai/v1/classify). Ours is GET local auto-router tiering — same path name, different method, body, backend, response. /v1/segment is POST, a Jina segmenter proxy (segment/route.ts, body {content, tokenizer?, return_tokens?, return_chunks?, max_chunk_length?} → segment.jina.ai). Ours is GET sentence-splitting + per-segment classification — entirely different semantics. The builder's "exact /v1/segment semantics NOT verified" limitation is now resolved: they diverge fundamentally. /v1/explain/routing is GET in both, but OmniRoute returns an object:"routing_explain" payload of recent routing EVENTS ring buffer + quality snapshot + otel stats (explain/routing/route.ts), not a per-prompt hypothetical decision. Only the last-served/events concept overlaps. Recommendation: split into three rows. Classify and segment stay MISSING as OmniRoute defines them (with a note that a local-introspection variant exists at the same path), explain → PARTIAL. Moving classify/segment to PARTIAL on name-only similarity would misstate parity.
Nits: (1) Model echo priority reversed (legacy_completions.go:toLegacyResponse): upstream model wins over requested; OmniRoute echoes the requested identifier (requestedModel ?? obj.model). For virtual-model requests the body names the concrete upstream model instead of what the client asked. Cheap fix (prefer req.Model when non-empty); OpenAI semantics arguably favour ours, so nit, not blocking. (2) isSegmentSpace comment overstates (legacy_completions.go): claims "Unicode whitespace" but checks only ' ', '\t', '\n', '\r' — e.g. NBSP after a period does not split. Fix the comment or use unicode.IsSpace. (3) Two silent-accept edges contradicting the file's "never silently dropped" principle: stop:"" yields [""] passed to the engine, and best_of:0 is accepted and ignored (only >1 is refused). Reject or document. (4) 502 paths use raw json.NewEncoder (handleClassify, handleExplainRouting) instead of the package's writeJSON — inconsistent, harmless.
Honest limitations — confirmed as stated: (1) not registered; (2) no EndpointLegacyCompletions kind; (3) no streaming/n>1/echo/suffix/logprobs/penalties/token-id prompts, no async-guardrail run, no TPM true-up/budget-overage header; (4) no per-target dry-run chain scoring (no such engine API exists — confirmed); (5) segment semantics now verified divergent (see Finding 2); (6) full-suite >10-min timeout not re-verified by the reviewer.
Process notes: review only: no edits to PARITY.md, LOG.md, CHECKLIST.md, guard.go, or server.go; no commit, no push, no remote added. Scratch copy and logs removed; live tree left exactly as found (builder's untracked files only).

The test proves the full chain live: below threshold → no header, no delivery; crossing $0.80/$1.00 → header X-Codvian-Budget-Warning: warn-budget-m819 + one signed delivery (event: budget.warning, pct: ~80, threshold: 0.8); repeat request above threshold → header but NO second delivery (edge-triggered, exactly 1 delivery total). Persistence/rollback: mutations go through s.configPersist with in-memory rollback on failure; dispatcher rebuilt from stored list after every mutation (nil dispatcher = no delivery). Proposed tier/scope + registration (coordinator): precedent: mutating config-persisting management routes with a secret/URL the server will act on are Tier 2 (e.g. POST /api/providers, DELETE /api/providers/{id}, DELETE /admin/keys/{id} — all TierAlwaysProtected, ["admin"]); read/mutate budget management is Tier 3 (e.g. GET/POST /api/usage/budget — TierManagement, ["admin"]).   // guard.go — guardRouteTable, add FIRST (register() panics on unclassified routes, guard.go:340-346):
  {Method: "POST", Path: "/api/webhooks", Tier: TierAlwaysProtected, Description: "Registers a webhook URL + event list (quota.exceeded, budget.warning): validates the URL (http/https only, no private/loopback targets), mints a wh_ id, persists to the config file and rebuilds the live dispatcher. Auth always required — it points the server at an operator-chosen URL and stores a signing secret.", Scopes: []string{"admin"}},
  {Method: "GET", Path: "/api/webhooks", Tier: TierManagement, Description: "Lists webhook registrations (id, url, events, has_secret only — secrets never returned). Management auth (admin scope) required.", Scopes: []string{"admin"}},
  {Method: "GET", Path: "/api/webhooks/{id}", Tier: TierManagement, Description: "One webhook registration by id (secret never returned); unknown id is 404. Management auth (admin scope) required.", Scopes: []string{"admin"}},
  {Method: "DELETE", Path: "/api/webhooks/{id}", Tier: TierAlwaysProtected, Description: "Removes a webhook registration, persists the config file and rebuilds the live dispatcher; unknown id is 404. Auth always required — it silently drops an operator alert channel.", Scopes: []string{"admin"}},
  // server.go — in the mux setup, after the /api/cache/stats line (~1320), AFTER the guard rows above land:
  s.registerWebhooksRoutes(mux)
Path templates must match byte-identically between guard rows and registerWebhooksRoutes (/api/webhooks, /api/webhooks/{id}).

Honest limitations: no live delivery verification against a real public webhook endpoint — the test uses an httptest localhost receiver seeded via config, because the API itself (correctly) rejects localhost targets. No retries by design; a down endpoint logs and drops the delivery. Localhost/private targets cannot be registered via the API (config file only); no operator override flag exists yet — the file documents this as the open follow-up. Edge-trigger state is in-memory per budget id (budgetWarningState): it resets on restart, so the first warn after a restart re-fires once; re-fire after a period reset works because spend drops below threshold first. Registration-time DNS check only: a hostname resolving to a public IP at registration could later resolve privately (no IP pinning at delivery time); delivery itself does no SSRF re-check. No PUT update endpoint; routes unregistered, so nothing is reachable yet.

M8.14 race fix (merge-gate blocking defect — independent reviewer FAIL 1b; closes the "M8.14 concurrent-map race" open follow-up above). Root cause: ComboStore.mu guarded only the writers; no reader of cfg.Routing.Groups / cfg.Routing.ChainStrategies ever held it, so a combo mirror write raced group traffic (fatal `concurrent map read and map write` in a non-race build).

Fix design (reviewer's minimal spec, one shared mutex — no redesign, no copy-on-write): a single `*sync.RWMutex` on *config.Config (`routingMu`, internal/config/config.go, with the lock-order comment at its declaration). Pointer, not value, so Config.Clone's `out := *c` shares the one mutex instead of copying it; nil means "not initialised yet" (DefaultConfig/Load initialise it; EnsureRoutingMu covers literal configs, called by NewComboStore). Lock order: ComboStore.mu -> routingMu, never the reverse; never held across disk I/O or a network call. Writers take the write lock inside mirrorGroup / mirrorStrategy / unmirror (internal/server/combos_api.go), including their rollback closures; the lock is released before any persist callback runs. Readers take the read lock or use the snapshot helpers (GroupMembers / GroupsSnapshot / ChainStrategy / ChainStrategiesSnapshot, all on *Config): groupChainFor + the engine ChainStrategy closure (cmd/codvian-router/main.go), GET /dashboard/groups (internal/server/groups.go, snapshot first), strategyForModel (internal/server/legacy_completions.go), Config.Clone (routing block only), the routingPersist copy+swap (main.go — copy, update application and pointer swap under one write lock, Save after unlock), and Save itself (Validate + Marshal under the read lock, file write/rename after unlock). Additional accessors found by grep beyond the reviewer's list and fixed too: the engine ChainStrategy closure (main.go:2675, live request path), the strategies feed (internal/server/server.go:5100 — reviewer said no lock site lived in server.go; one did, now snapshot under the read lock), NewComboStore seeding (snapshot under the read lock), and Save's Validate/Marshal reads (covered via Save's own read lock; Validate's doc notes it must run single-threaded or under the read lock). Residual, NOT fixed here (pre-existing, out of this defect's scope): the cfg.Routing pointer swap in routingPersist is serialised against the locked accessors, but other scalar readers of RoutingConfig fields (e.g. main.go dashboard status reads of GlobalStrategy, dbexport.go) still deref cfg.Routing without the lock; and routingPersist's Save-after-swap reads the new block under Save's own read lock, which serialises it against mirrors. No behaviour change: group routing still resolves, combos CRUD unchanged (all 20 M8.14 slice tests pass, incl. round-trip + persist rollback).

Soak test: internal/server/combos_race_test.go TestCombosRaceSoak (3 s bounded run; 4 writer goroutines driving the live combos HTTP handler — create / patch-to-fusion (record-only, drops the strategy mirror) / patch-back / get / delete — against 4 reader goroutines: GroupMembers + GroupsSnapshot, Clone, ChainStrategy + ChainStrategiesSnapshot + strategyForModel, and groupsFeed on the same cfg). Toggle evidence (scratch copy under $HOME/tmp/gowork/codvian-router-toggle, since deleted): same tree with the four lock methods stubbed to no-ops (every access unlocked, exactly as the pre-fix code) — the test FAILS: 28 `WARNING: DATA RACE` reports (mirrorGroup map write vs GroupMembers / Clone / ChainStrategy reads) and then `fatal error: concurrent map iteration and map write` in Config.Clone, exit 1. With the real mutex: `go test -race -run TestCombosRaceSoak` ok in 4.1 s.
