M8.14 race fix, round 2 (independent re-reviewer FAIL on the round-1 fix: core M8.14 race fixed, all suites green, but two residuals proven real).

1. dbexport races the combo mirrors (BLOCKING, proven panic). handleDBExport built the payload with `Routing: cfg.Routing` and `enc.Encode(payload)` ranged the LIVE Groups / ChainStrategies maps with no lock. Fix: the encoder now encodes `cfg.RoutingSnapshot()` — one deep copy of the whole routing block under the routing read lock (internal/config/config.go: RoutingSnapshot + unexported cloneRoutingLocked, shared with Clone so the two copies can never drift). The copy is a true deep copy of every map the encoder ranges inside Routing: Groups, ChainStrategies, ChainWeights (inner maps too), ChainCooldownModes, RefusalChains, ModelEquivalents, StrategyTrees (via strategytree.Clone), plus the ContextFallbackModels slice and the WeightedWeights / ModelLockout (with its error-code slice) / WaitBudgets pointers (WaitBudgets and RoutingWeights hold scalars only; ModelLockout's only slice is ErrorCodes — verified by field inspection). Hardening found while verifying "any other map the payload touches": Prices and ProviderPrices are also copied now (local copyModelPrice, mirroring the unexported ModelPrice.clone, fresh *float64 pointers). ProviderPrices is written live by the pricing PATCH under pricingMu (pricing_api.go), so its copy takes that lock; Prices has no live writer. Guardrails (pointer, no live writer — verified by grep) and the AutoRouter maps (no live writer — verified by grep) stay shared reads, as do cfg.Providers (startup-only appends) and len(cfg.GatewayKeys). Lock order unchanged: the routing snapshot lock is released before encoding, and pricingMu is taken on its own for the CPU-only copy, never nested with routingMu.

2. cfg.Routing pointer swap vs unlocked readers (NEEDS-FIX, proven real under -race). routingPersist swaps cfg.Routing under the write lock (main.go), but strategiesFeed / cooldownFeed (via routingConfig), strategyForModel, groupsFeed, Clone, chainFor and contextFallbackChain all loaded the pointer unlocked. Fix: the same RoutingSnapshot accessor is now the whole-block read path everywhere — routingConfig() returns a snapshot (so strategiesFeed and cooldownFeed both read from it; cooldown.go itself needed no edit), strategyForModel reads one snapshot for both the per-chain entry and GlobalStrategy, groupsFeed dropped its pre-lock `s.cfg.Routing == nil` check (GroupsSnapshot takes the nil check under the lock), and main.go chainFor (ModelEquivalents) and contextFallbackChain read snapshots per request. The nil checks in GroupMembers / GroupsSnapshot / ChainStrategy / ChainStrategiesSnapshot moved inside the lock. Clone needed one more change than the spec listed: its `out := *c` struct copy reads the Routing pointer, so the read lock now covers the struct copy AND the routing deep copy (cloneRoutingLocked called with the lock held; no caller holds routingMu while calling Clone — callers are danger.go / keystatus.go handlers — so no deadlock). The routingMu field comment now states the rule: never deref cfg.Routing directly; whole-block readers use RoutingSnapshot. Left alone deliberately: main.go:2403 (`routing := cfg.Routing`, engine-build, single-threaded startup) and main.go:3916 (startup banner print) — no concurrent writer exists at those points; Validate's `c.Routing` read (config.go:1135) runs under Save's read lock or single-threaded at load; NewComboStore's nil check (combos_api.go:193) runs at construction before the config is shared.

routingPersist deep-copy assessment (reviewer's open question): NOT done, deliberately. routingPersist deep-copies only the fields it mutates (ChainStrategies / WeightedWeights / ChainCooldownModes) — Groups, ChainWeights, RefusalChains, ModelEquivalents, StrategyTrees share the old block's maps. Deep-copying them is not trivially safe: a combo mirror landing between the swap and a failed-Save rollback writes into the new block's maps, and with shared maps that write survives the rollback (it is a live user action with its own persist, so surviving is the correct outcome); with fresh maps the same write would go into the discarded block and be lost in memory while the file keeps it — a new lost-update window. And since routingPersist never mutates Groups (or any shared map), there is nothing for the rollback to restore in them. OPEN FOLLOW-UP (not blocking): routingPersist in cmd/codvian-router/main.go:3240-3298 — decide the intended rollback semantics for a mirror landing between swap and rollback (keep-survives vs restore-pristine) before touching the shared maps; do not deep-copy them as a drive-by.

Stale comment tails corrected (comment-only, verified against the wired tree): logs_api.go:33-34 now says tier/scope are wired at registration (TierManagement, scope "admin", guard.go:167, server.go:1347) instead of "proposed… the coordinator assigns them"; quotas.go header no longer carries the COORDINATOR PATCH paragraph ("fixture stays RED until that patch lands") — it now states the field is live (meStatusUsageWindow.TotalRequests, keystatus.go:102, accumulated `w.TotalRequests += sl.Rows` in usageWindow's slices loop, :224) and the fixture asserts it.

Soak test (kept): internal/server/dbexport_race_test.go TestDBExportRaceSoak (3 s bounded run; 4 combo-writer goroutines on the live handler + a routingPersist-style swap loop under the write lock — copy, global-strategy flip, swap, rollback every 8th swap — against 5 reader goroutines: the export encoder through POST /admin/db/export with an admin key + EXPORT, the strategies + cooldown feeds through the dashboard routes, and strategyForModel + Clone + GroupMembers / GroupsSnapshot + groupsFeed). Toggle evidence (scratch copy under $HOME/tmp/gowork/codvian-router-toggle, since deleted): the four reader files reverted to direct `cfg.Routing` loads (dbexport, routingConfig + strategiesFeed loop, strategyForModel, groupsFeed nil check) — the test FAILS: 43 `WARNING: DATA RACE` reports and `panic: reflect: call of reflect.Value.Set on zero Value` (the re-reviewer's exact panic), FAIL in 0.138 s; 27 of the race stacks implicate `json.(*Encoder).Encode()` at dbexport.go:199, the export encoder. With the fix: `go test -race -run TestDBExportRaceSoak` ok (4.05 s; passed twice more in a -count=2 run). The first post-fix run caught one more real race the spec had not named — Clone's unlocked struct copy vs the swap — fixed by holding the read lock across the copy (see above). No-behaviour-change proof: a temporary config-hash test (deleted afterwards) exported one fixed config in both trees — sha256 of the config section identical pre/post fix: 81968b2d11cd376c29432a1e9e16d8940be302776f5f9e862ee1f8f9899c00bf.

Verification (Go 1.27.4, -count=1, TMPDIR=$HOME/.gotmp): go build ./... ok; go vet ./internal/config/ ./internal/server/ ./cmd/codvian-router/ ok; gofmt -l clean on every file touched; full ./internal/server/ suite 915 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP (165.6 s — 914 in round 1 + the new TestDBExportRaceSoak); ./internal/config/ 96 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP; ./cmd/codvian-router/ 57 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP; -race TestCombosRaceSoak + TestDBExportRaceSoak, -count=2: all PASS (13.3 s). No commit, no push, no remote, no GitHub writes.

## M8.14 race fix, round 3 — re-reviewer FAIL drivers A + B, pricing hazard, dbexport assessment (2026-11-19)

Fix builder, third blocking round. Verdicts: item 1 FIXED, item 2 FIXED, item 3 FIXED, item 4 assessed — comment fixed, both sub-items OPEN FOLLOW-UP (no clean lock exists; see below). No commit, no push, no remote, no GitHub writes. Files touched only: internal/config/config.go, internal/server/combos_api.go, danger.go, keystatus.go, pricing_api.go, dbexport.go, internal/server/race_round3_test.go (new), docs/LOG.md (this entry). docs/PARITY.md, docs/PLAN.md, docs/CHECKLIST.md, guard.go untouched; ComboStore.mu -> routingMu order unchanged; no slice behaviour changed.

Item 1 — NewComboStore unlocked nil check (combos_api.go:191): FIXED. Dropped the `cfg.Routing == nil` early return entirely. The round-2 justification ("runs at construction before the config is shared") was false, as the reviewer said: combosStore() (server.go:1186-1193) builds the store LAZILY on the first live /v1/combos request, concurrent with serving, racing routingPersist's swap (main.go:3293-3294) and mirrorGroup's cfg.Routing creation (combos_api.go:491-492). GroupsSnapshot() / ChainStrategy() already take the routing read lock and handle a nil block (nil snapshot / absent), so a nil Routing now seeds nothing instead of crashing. No other code depended on the early return: the only production caller is combosStore(), and every test caller passes a non-nil Routing (comboTestConfig, groupStack) — verified by grep. EnsureRoutingMu runs first, serialised by Server.combosMu around the lazy construction.

Item 2 — whole-struct config restores swap Routing (and the mutex) unlocked: FIXED, all 6 sites (grep for `*s.cfg = ` across internal/ + cmd/ found exactly these; UnmarshalJSON's `*c = Config(alias)` is single-threaded Load, not a live restore, left alone). New method Config.RestoreFrom(src) (internal/config/config.go): restores every field from src while preserving the LIVE mutex objects and writing Routing under the routing write lock. Call sites: danger.go:126 (revoke-all rollback), :167 (reset — `*s.cfg = *defs`, the worst site: DefaultConfig's fresh routingMu/pricesMu used to replace the live mutexes), :169 (reset rollback), keystatus.go:659 (update rollback), :758 (delete rollback), :870 (rotate rollback). Lock order on every restore path: dangerMu / keyMu -> pricesMu -> routingMu — the same direction Save nests them in, so no cycle: no path takes pricesMu/routingMu and then dangerMu, keyMu, pricingMu or ComboStore.mu (mirrors release routingMu before any persist runs; routingPersist releases it before Save).
- Design correction found by the probe (two real bugs in the first RestoreFrom draft, both fixed here): (a) doing `*c = *src` under the locks and then overwriting the mutex fields is FATAL — a concurrent RLockRouting that read c.routingMu mid-copy grabbed the FOREIGN mutex and its RUnlock then unlocked a different mutex ("RUnlock of unlocked RWMutex", caught by TestDangerKeystatusRestoreRace); (b) even writing the SAME live pointer value is a race — the struct copy writes the routingMu/pricesMu fields while RLockRouting/LockRouting read those pointers unlocked (a reader must read the pointer before it can take the mutex, so no lock ordering can make the write safe). The shipped method therefore copies field-by-field via reflection, skipping every unexported field (today: routingMu, pricesMu) — a struct assignment is never used, the mutex pointer fields are never written at all, and a field added to Config later is restored automatically. src must be caller-private (Clone snapshot or fresh DefaultConfig); the method documents that precondition.

Item 3 — Clone/Save read ProviderPrices without pricingMu: FIXED, with a new config-level mutex (config cannot import the server package, so server pricingMu cannot be taken in Clone/Save). New field Config.pricesMu (*sync.RWMutex, same pointer-sharing rule as routingMu: Clone's `out := *c` shares it; DefaultConfig/Load initialise it; EnsurePricesMu covers literal configs) plus nil-safe Lock/RLock methods and ProviderPricesSnapshot() (deep copy under the prices read lock). Lock order, now documented at the routingMu declaration (the old "pricingMu and routingMu are never nested" phrasing was false and is removed): pricingMu -> pricesMu -> routingMu, one-directional, reverse never occurs. Clone snapshots ProviderPrices under the prices read lock BEFORE taking the routing read lock; Save holds the prices read lock across Validate + Marshal and takes the routing read lock inside it; the pricing PATCH (pricing_api.go) takes the prices WRITE lock for its ProviderPrices snapshot + apply and re-takes it for rollback — but releases it BEFORE configPersist, because Save takes the prices READ lock and holding the write lock across the persist would deadlock. That release is safe: the only ProviderPrices writer is this PATCH path and PATCHes are serialised by pricingMu (still held), so no writer can interleave between apply and persist — only readers, which is exactly what the read lock serialises against. LoadPriceBook now ranges a ProviderPricesSnapshot too (its only live caller is startup, but the snapshot costs nothing). Validate's doc now requires the routing + prices read locks (Save takes both).

Item 4 — dbexport assessment: comment fixed, both sub-items OPEN FOLLOW-UP. There is no literal "startup-only" comment in dbexport.go in this tree — the false invariant was implicit in the unlocked range — so the fix is an explicit correction comment at the range (dbexport.go): cfg.Providers is NOT startup-only, configProviderStore.Save (cmd/codvian-router/main.go:676-679) replaces the whole slice at runtime on every live provider add/remove, and no clean lock exists — the provider store has no mutex of its own and cfg.Providers has no guarding mutex anywhere (every other reader ranges it unlocked too), so a snapshot lock needs a new mutex wired through the store and all readers (a redesign, in cmd/ + server, outside this round's scope). len(cfg.GatewayKeys) likewise: no single mutex guards the slice — keystatus mutates it under keyMu, danger revoke-all under dangerMu, and the bootstrap issuer (main.go:3216, via config.AddGatewayKey) appends with NO lock — so snapshotting under keyMu alone would still race revoke-all and bootstrap. A real fix needs one guard for the slice (or a count accessor on Config) wired through all three writers. Residual noted for the coordinator: RestoreFrom still writes the Providers/GatewayKeys slice headers (unavoidable in a whole-struct restore without the item-4 redesign). Correction (round-3 re-review, nit 1): "only Routing, ProviderPrices and the mutexes are serialised by this round" was overstated — ProviderPrices is serialised vs Clone/Save/PATCH but NOT vs dbexport (pre-fix: dbexport ranged it under pricingMu only, unserialised vs RestoreFrom's pricesMu-only write), and Prices was serialised vs nothing (Clone ranged it with no lock held, config.go:2760-2763 pre-fix; dbexport ranged it unlocked, dbexport.go:210-216 pre-fix). Both price legs are closed by the nit-closure fix below; the Providers/GatewayKeys legs remain open. Clone's GatewayKeys/Providers reads race concurrent mutations for the same reason — pre-existing, same follow-up.

Probes (kept): internal/server/race_round3_test.go — TestCombosLazyNilRoutingRace (nil Routing + lazy combosStore() construction vs a routingPersist-style swap loop flipping nil/populated + Clone/snapshot readers, 2 s), TestDangerKeystatusRestoreRace (FAILING persist forces every RestoreFrom on every call: revoke-all + reset + key update/delete/rotate rollbacks vs direct mirrorGroup/mirrorStrategy/unmirror under ComboStore.mu + the swap loop + GroupsSnapshot/ChainStrategy readers, 2 s), TestPricingCloneSaveRace (concurrent PATCH + Clone + direct Save to the same file + GET pricing, real Save as the persist, 2 s). Two deliberate probe narrowings, both documented in the tests: in the item-2 probe the danger/key handlers are serialised against each other by a test-side mutex, and mirrors run directly instead of over HTTP with no groupsFeed — because concurrent danger+key mutation and the HTTP combo view / groupsFeed all range cfg.GatewayKeys / cfg.Providers unlocked, which is the item-4 race, not the RestoreFrom race; the probe isolates the Routing/mutex race, and the toggle proves it still catches the pre-fix code.
Toggle evidence (scratch copy under $HOME/tmp/gowork/codvian-router-toggle, since deleted; pristine files restored between toggles, one toggle at a time): item 1 — NewComboStore reverted to the `cfg.Routing == nil` early return: FAIL, 1 DATA RACE (swap-loop write vs NewComboStore's unlocked read), 2.129 s. Item 2 — all 6 RestoreFrom calls reverted to plain `*s.cfg = *snapshot` / `*s.cfg = *defs`: FAIL, 15 DATA RACE reports, 0.105 s. Item 3 — ProviderPricesSnapshot unlocked + Save without the prices lock + PATCH without the prices lock: FAIL, 6 DATA RACE reports (PATCH map write vs Clone/Save reads), 0.537 s. With the fixes: all three tests PASS under -race.

Verification (Go 1.27.4, -count=1, TMPDIR=$HOME/.gotmp): go build ./... ok; go vet ./internal/config/ ./internal/server/ ./cmd/codvian-router/ ok; gofmt -l clean on every file touched; the three new tests under -race ok (7.379 s); TestCombosRaceSoak + TestDBExportRaceSoak under -race still PASS (7.230 s); owning-slice targeted run (TestCombos|TestDanger|TestRevokeAll|TestReset|TestKey|TestM815|TestPricing|TestDBExport|TestConfig) ok — server 23.695 s, config 0.226 s; full ./internal/server/ suite 918 tests PASS / 0 FAIL (200.446 s); full ./internal/config/ suite 96 tests PASS / 0 FAIL (0.225 s). No behaviour change: combos CRUD, danger reset/revoke-all, keystatus update/delete/rotate, pricing PATCH and dbexport all exercised by their owning slice tests above, all green.

Open follow-ups (for the coordinator, not blocking this round): (1) Providers guard — one mutex for cfg.Providers wired through configProviderStore + every reader (groups.go, combos_api.go providerByID, dbexport.go, Clone, RestoreFrom) — until then RestoreFrom's Providers-header write and every unlocked Providers range remain racy. (2) GatewayKeys guard — one mutex (or a Config count accessor) wired through keyMu paths, dangerMu paths and the bootstrap issuer — until then len(cfg.GatewayKeys) in dbexport and Clone's GatewayKeys copy remain racy against concurrent key mutations.

## M8.14 race fix, round 3 nit closure — dbexport + Prices vs RestoreFrom (2026-11-19)

Fix builder, pre-gate nit closure. Verdicts: nit 2 (dbexport leg) FIXED, nit 1 (LOG correction + Prices residual) FIXED, Clone leg FIXED (the safe hold existed — see below). No commit, no push, no remote, no GitHub writes. Files touched only: internal/config/config.go, internal/server/dbexport.go, docs/LOG.md (this entry). docs/PARITY.md, docs/PLAN.md, docs/CHECKLIST.md, guard.go, server.go untouched; lock order pricingMu -> pricesMu -> routingMu unchanged, one-directional; Providers/GatewayKeys redesign NOT attempted.

Nit 2 — dbexport price legs: FIXED. Note: ProviderPricesSnapshot() already existed from round 3 — the task's "add it" was already done, so this fix reused it and added the missing half: PricesSnapshot() (config.go:2302, deep copy under the prices read lock, nil when no model-level prices are configured), plus unexported Locked variants both snapshots share (providerPricesSnapshotLocked :2250, pricesSnapshotLocked :2269) so a caller that already holds the read lock never takes it twice. dbexport.go:187-188 now takes both snapshots instead of the manual pricingMu-guarded copy (Prices was ranged unlocked, ProviderPrices under pricingMu only — both unserialised vs RestoreFrom, which writes both headers under pricesMu alone). The stale comment above it is corrected: it said ProviderPrices "is written by the pricing PATCH under pricingMu" and "Prices has no live writer" — both false post-round-3, since RestoreFrom also writes both under pricesMu only. The now-dead copyModelPrice helper (only used by the removed manual copy) is deleted. Snapshot semantics match the old code for the payload: both helpers return nil when their map is absent where the old code built empty non-nil maps, and both fields are omitempty — the export-output comparison below proves the bytes are identical.

Nit 1 — LOG correction: FIXED. The round-3 item-4 sentence above is corrected in place (ProviderPrices serialised vs Clone/Save/PATCH but NOT vs dbexport pre-fix; Prices serialised vs nothing pre-fix, with the pre-fix file:line citations). The pricesMu field comment (config.go, "Prices has NO live writer and is not guarded by this mutex") was false for the same reason and is corrected too: RestoreFrom is Prices' one live writer (header write under the prices write lock) and Prices is now guarded by pricesMu via PricesSnapshot/Clone. The lock-order comment now says Clone snapshots both price maps under pricesMu before the routing lock and holds the prices read lock across the whole Clone.

Clone leg — FIXED, no redesign needed. Clone (config.go:2613) now takes the prices read lock at the top and holds it (defer RUnlockPrices) across the whole Clone, snapshotting both price maps through the Locked helpers and assigning out.Prices from the snapshot (:2837) instead of ranging c.Prices unlocked. The hold is safe, verified by inspection, not assumed: (a) no order reversal — the only other lock Clone takes is the routing read lock inside the hold (pricesMu -> routingMu, the documented order); (b) no nested-RLock deadlock — Clone takes pricesMu nowhere else, and the snapshots go through the Locked helpers precisely because calling the locking Snapshot methods under a held read lock would deadlock when a writer (RestoreFrom, pricing PATCH) is waiting; (c) no cycle — no Clone caller holds routingMu, pricesMu or pricingMu (danger.go holds dangerMu, keystatus.go holds keyMu, tests hold nothing), and Clone never takes pricingMu, dangerMu or keyMu; (d) no I/O under the lock — CPU-only deep copying, the same class as Save holding the prices read lock across Validate + Marshal; the hold only briefly delays a RestoreFrom or PATCH writer. Side benefit: the `out := *c` header copy now also runs under the prices read lock, so the Prices/ProviderPrices header reads are serialised vs RestoreFrom's header writes too (pre-fix Clone's header read under the routing lock alone raced the writer's pricesMu write — caught by the Toggle B probe at config.go:2595 in scratch).

Probes (scratch only — internal/server/nit_probe_test.go in a scratch copy under $HOME/tmp/gowork/nit-scratch, since deleted, never committed): three -race probes, 3 s each. The writer performs exactly RestoreFrom's price-header writes (alternating two prebuilt price-map pairs, headers swapped, maps never mutated in place) under exactly the lock RestoreFrom holds for them (LockPrices/UnlockPrices) — and deliberately nothing else. It does NOT use a literal RestoreFrom call: a real RestoreFrom also writes the Providers/GatewayKeys headers, which dbexport and Clone read unlocked, so no probe with a real RestoreFrom writer can pass until the item-4 redesign lands; this probe isolates the price legs, and the Providers/GatewayKeys legs stay OPEN (see below). Readers: the real export route (POST /admin/db/export, admin key + EXPORT), a Clone loop, and snapshot loops.
Toggle evidence (one toggle at a time, pristine files restored between toggles): post-fix, all three probes PASS under -race, standalone and in-sequence, 0 races (10.115 s in-sequence). Toggle A — dbexport reverted to the manual pricingMu-guarded copy: TestNitDbexportPricesRace FAILS, 8 DATA RACE reports (stacks at the reverted copy, dbexport.go:210/211/215/216 in scratch), TestNitClonePricesRace PASSES with 0 races — the dbexport leg isolated. Toggle B — Clone reverted to the round-3 shape (ProviderPricesSnapshot at the top + unlocked Prices range), dbexport fixed: TestNitClonePricesRace FAILS, 10 DATA RACE reports (stacks at the unlocked Prices range, config.go:2811-2813 in scratch, plus the header read at :2595), TestNitDbexportPricesRace PASSES with 0 races — the Clone leg isolated. One probe caveat, recorded honestly: TestNitPricesCombinedRace is timing-sensitive in-sequence — under Toggle A it PASSED once when run third in the same process while failing standalone twice with 8 races (the Clone loop starves the export loop on this 1.5-CPU VM); the isolated tests are the deterministic evidence, and the combined probe fails standalone under both toggles and passes standalone post-fix.
No-behaviour-change proof: the scratch probe's TestNitExportConfigHash exports one fixed seeded config and hashes the config section (exported_at stripped) — sha256 identical pre-fix (Toggle A) and post-fix: 9d613e86c15d3062cc1a568cbc5fcc24be923811f39d7668ebff7575bb20b75f.

## M8.8 Batch API — builder report (2026-11-19)

- Built: OpenAI-compatible Batch API (the one M8 slice never built; CHECKLIST row 365). `internal/server/batches.go` (Batch store + worker) + `internal/server/batches_api.go` (handlers) + `internal/server/batches_api_test.go` (13 tests) + 6 fixtures under `tests/fixtures/v1/m8.8/` + additive `CreateServerGenerated` in `internal/files/store.go` (server-generated `batch_output` / `batch_error` purposes; upload validation untouched).
- Routes (all Tier MANAGEMENT, scope "chat" — a batch IS a set of chat completions; no separate "batch" scope exists in the codebase): POST /v1/batches, GET /v1/batches, GET /v1/batches/{id}, POST /v1/batches/{id}/cancel, DELETE /v1/batches/{id}, DELETE /v1/batches/delete-completed. Guard rows + `server.go` registrations wired (170 → 176 routes); `scripts/gen-api-docs.py` re-run (API_REFERENCE.md, llm.txt, tryit.html, m8.21 routes.json); `guard_test.go` expected list + tier counts updated (MANAGEMENT 141 → 147).
- Execution is REAL: the worker validates every JSONL line (custom_id required + unique, method POST, url /v1/chat/completions, body with model + ≥1 message; ≤50 line errors, 1-based, duplicates ≤10 reported) and executes each valid line sequentially through `s.engine.Chat` — the same engine `handleChatCompletions` uses. Output lines are OpenAI-shaped (`{id, custom_id, response: {status_code, request_id, body: {chat completion}}}`); failures go to the error file (`{id, custom_id, error: {code, message}}`). Output/error files are written back via the Files store (purposes `batch_output` / `batch_error`). Zero valid lines → status failed, errors code `invalid_input_file`. Cancel really stops the worker: cancelling → cancelled, in-flight `Chat` aborts on ctx cancel, no further lines start (test asserts exactly 1 engine call after cancelling a 3-line batch); 409 on terminal cancel/delete-running.
- Persistence: `<data-dir>/batches/batches.json` (atomic temp+rename+fsync, `{next_seq, batches}` wrapper); a batch still running at restart is marked failed/`server_restart` on load — never silently re-run (would double-spend) or left in_progress. List order is a persisted creation sequence (Seq desc), NOT (created_at, id): same-second ties made the first version flaky (TestBatchesListLimit failed once under race) — fixed with Seq, passes at count=5.
- Scope decision: "chat" for all six routes (reads and writes share it — the guard table has no read/write split for this shape); batch records are owner-scoped via `callerKeyID` (foreign ids answer 404, never 403).
- Pricing/ledger decision (honest gap, no invented numbers): lines execute through `engine.Chat`, so the ledger records them at the SYNC rate. The 50% batch rate (OmniRoute `batchCost = syncCost * 0.5`) is NOT applied: it needs a batch flag threaded through `provider.ChatRequest` → `router.Attempt` → `attemptCost`, a hot-path change outside this slice. The Batch object reports real summed token usage only; no discounted cost is claimed anywhere. Follow-up seam named for the coordinator.
- Other honest gaps: (a) input-file ownership CANNOT be enforced — `files.File` records no owner (M8.7 design; the Files API itself lists every file to any key), so any authenticated key can use any file as batch input. OmniRoute denies null-owner/foreign input files (GHSA-2jm2-mpx8-6523); that check needs an owner column on the Files store — a coordinator decision, not this slice. (b) Batch lines bypass the HTTP handler's per-request gates (guardrails, key budgets, traffic limits); batch create is the admission point and per-line spend is still ledger-recorded by the engine. (c) No expiry sweeper — `expired` only lands if a batch finishes past `expires_at`. (d) Validated lines are held in memory until executed; output/error lines stream to temp files on disk.
- Proof: `go test ./internal/server/ -race -count=1` PASS (931 top-level PASS, 0 FAIL, no data races); `go test ./internal/files/ -race -count=1` ok; `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt -l` clean. Live smoke through `srv.Handler()` with a gateway key: POST /v1/batches + GET /v1/batches/{id} → 200, no key → 401. TOGGLE-VERIFY: neutralising `s.engine.Chat` in the worker → `TestBatchesCreateCompletesWithRealOutput` FAILS (usage 0/0/0); restored code passes.
- PARITY.md NOT flipped here (Batches row 1072 stays MISSING; Area 11 batch/flex pricing row stays MISSING — no batch-rate pricing): left for the exit gate/review. CHECKLIST.md row 365 marked Built ✅, Verified blank.

## M8.8 Batch API — independent review (2026-11-19): PASS, no blocking findings

- Reviewer re-ran everything: `go test ./internal/server/ -race -count=1` → 926 top-level PASS, 0 FAIL, no races; `go test ./internal/files/ -race -count=1` → 7 PASS, 0 FAIL; build/vet/gofmt clean; batch subset 13/13 PASS. Live smoke re-run (POST /v1/batches → 200, GET /v1/batches/{id} → 200, no key → 401). Own toggle: neutralising `s.engine.Chat` fails TestBatchesCreateCompletesWithRealOutput; restored byte-for-byte (6 sha256 match, git status/diff identical to pre-review).
- Verified: worker really executes via `s.engine.Chat` (batches.go, same engine as chat completions); cancel really stops work (ctx checked before every line, partial results published, status cancelled); no discounted cost anywhere in the batch path (only a comment stating the 50% rate is NOT applied); ownership gap real as described (files.File has no owner, Store.List returns every file unfiltered) — NOT blocking for this slice (pre-existing M8.7 design; fixing needs an owner column on the Files store, coordinator decision); persistence + restart behavior confirmed; scope `chat` blessed (matches Files/Responses convention); no fake data or overclaims; PARITY rows correctly left MISSING.

- Nits (non-blocking, carried to the exit gate, not fixed here): (1) GET /v1/batches/delete-completed answers 405 rather than 404 — 405 is arguably the correct semantic for a known path with a wrong method; leave as is. (2) New batches carry non-nil empty usage at creation; OpenAI returns usage: null until terminal — cosmetic shape difference, exit-gate follow-up. (3) batchVisible: auth-relaxed caller ("") sees every batch — consistent with this server's auth-relaxed convention. (4) Admitted gaps carried forward: no expiry sweeper, validated lines held in memory, batch lines bypass per-request guardrails/budget gates.

## M9.6 MCP client-side hardening (builder, 2026-11-19) — DONE

- Changed (internal/mcptools/ ONLY; no other slice's files touched — parallel M9.0/M9.1 files in the tree are theirs, not mine): new controls.go (Controls, ToolUsage, UsageReportingServer, CostEstimate, ExecutionResult, BudgetStatus, wildcard matcher, budget reserve, SetControls/Controls/ResetBudget/BudgetStatus) + mcptools.go wiring (NewWithControls, ExecuteDetailed choke point, Stats denial counters, Snapshot Controls/Budget) + controls_test.go (15 new tests).
- Choke point: Registry.ExecuteDetailed — every execution path funnels through it (direct Execute, agent-loop dispatchOne incl. mcp_tools_execute and direct-mode tool calls). Order per call: resolve against the discovered snapshot → allowlist → spend budget → dispatch. A refused call never contacts the server (proven by counting-handler tests asserting 0 dispatches).
- Allowlist: patterns match "server__tool" (glob "*", e.g. "calc__*", "*"), plain tool name (any server), or the discovered qualified name. Fail-closed once controls are engaged: empty Allow denies everything; Deny overrides Allow. Deliberate split (documented in controls.go): legacy New() engages NO controls (allow-all, pre-M9.6 behaviour) so the existing internal/server agent-loop wiring and its tests keep working; the hardened path NewWithControls/SetControls is deny-all from engagement. The config-file plumbing (MCPConfig fields + server.go wiring to NewWithControls) is OUTSIDE this slice's file scope — Controls is JSON-tagged so config.MCPConfig can embed it verbatim; until that wiring lands, a production registry built with New() is unhardened. Named follow-up for the coordinator, not hidden.
- Spend controls: per-registry budget (the registry is the session for one gateway process; a fresh Registry is a fresh budget, ResetBudget restarts the window in place): MaxCalls (N+1th dispatch refused before contact) + MaxDuration wall-clock budget from controls engagement / last ResetBudget (a call starting after the budget is spent is refused). Budget reserve is check-and-increment under one mutex (race-safe). Denials counted separately (Stats AllowlistDenied/BudgetDenied/DeniedCalls) and surfaced in Snapshot.
- Cost estimate (honest, no invented numbers): the MCP tools/call wire format carries no usage or price, so a plain ToolServer yields CostEstimate{UsageKnown:false} — cost UNKNOWN. A server that knows its usage implements the optional UsageReportingServer interface; its token counts are reported verbatim, and a USD figure ONLY when the server itself reports one (HasCost). USD SPEND CAP IS NOT ENFORCEABLE: there is no price table for external MCP tools anywhere in the codebase, so no dollar cap was faked — MaxCalls + MaxDuration are the real enforceable caps. A USD cap needs a per-tool price source (operator-configured price table or server-reported cost on every call) — coordinator decision, not this slice.
- What is NOT enforced (honest gaps): (1) mcp_tools_get listing is NOT allowlist-filtered — the model can see tools it cannot execute (discovery only; execution is the enforced surface; filtering the dashboard-consumed list is M9.7's call). (2) No per-request/per-key budget — budget is per-registry; a per-request budget needs a child registry per request (possible: same servers, fresh controls) or a session object, not built here. (3) Wall-clock budget is checked before dispatch, not mid-call — a single slow call can overrun the budget (the existing 30 s stdio per-call timeout still bounds it). (4) UsageReportingServer is only implemented by test doubles today — no real server reports usage, so in production cost is UNKNOWN until a server does.
- Proof: `go test ./internal/mcptools/ -race -count=1` PASS — 38 top-level PASS / 0 FAIL, no races (15 new M9.6 tests + 23 pre-existing incl. both M1 regressions: TestStdioServerSlowHandshakeTimeoutDoesNotPanic PASS, TestDiscoverThousandColliding64CharNamesTerminates PASS). `go vet` clean, `gofmt -l` clean, `go build ./...` clean. Server-package MCP tests re-run (read-only check, files untouched): TestChatCompletionsMCPLoopRunsInProcess, TestChatCompletionsMCPClientToolPassThrough, TestChatCompletionsMCPLoopCapHeader, TestHandleMCP* — all PASS, so legacy New() compat holds.
- TOGGLE-VERIFY: neutralising the allowlist check (`if false && !allowedByControls(...)`) → TestAllowlistUnlistedToolDeniedBeforeDispatch, TestAllowlistDenyByDefault, TestAllowlistDenyOverridesAllow, TestAgentLoopEnforcesAllowlist all FAIL; restored byte-for-byte (no TOGGLE-VERIFY marker left in the tree).
- PARITY.md NOT flipped here (Area 9 "Spend controls on MCP tools" row stays PARTIAL until the exit gate reviews: USD cap unenforceable without a price source + config-file wiring pending). CHECKLIST.md row 387 marked Built ✅, Verified blank.

## M9.1 MCP SECURITY BASELINE (builder, 2026-11-19)

- Built `internal/mcp/scopes.go` (own 9-scope set, NOT OmniRoute's 33: `mcp:read:status`, `mcp:read:providers`, `mcp:read:models`, `mcp:manage:routing`, `mcp:manage:cost`, `mcp:manage:cache`, `mcp:manage:memory`, `mcp:manage:ops`, `mcp:destructive`; per-tool assignment — 3 tools map to their read scope; `*` + trailing-`*` wildcard matching, parent scope does NOT cover children, mid-string `*` is literal; `CheckScopes` AND semantics with named missing scopes; `RegisterToolScopes` seam for M9.3/M9.4 — panics on unknown/empty scopes so a typo can never silently grant/deny). Enforcement ON by default with NO off switch anywhere on the server (deliberate divergence from OmniRoute's OFF default, featureFlags.ts:127-136); denial is JSON-RPC -32002 (ScopeDeniedCode), distinct from guardrail -32001 and -32602. Dispatch order in `internal/mcp/mcp.go`: scope check → guardrail → dispatch → audit; unknown tools skip the scope check and keep -32602.
- Built `internal/mcp/identity.go` (`ClientIdentity`: `LocalOperator()` full `*` grant, `GatewayKeyClient(keyID, scopes)` — id only, never key material, scopes defensively copied — `Anonymous()` no scopes; `HandleWithIdentity` per-request override for M9.2, server default untouched). STDIO TRUST DECISION: stdio's only caller is the local operator (same user/machine/filesystem access as the router itself — it can already read the config's provider keys), so a stdio scope restriction would be theatre; stdio therefore carries the full grant, and this is documented as safe ONLY for stdio. Remote transports MUST use gateway-key identities — a remote caller defaulting to LocalOperator would be privilege escalation; reviewer should verify in M9.2.
- Built `internal/mcp/audit.go` (append-only JSONL `<data-dir>/mcp/audit.jsonl`, dir 0700/file 0600, O_APPEND single-write + fsync per entry under a mutex, following the `internal/files/store.go` pattern — JSONL, not SQLite: no SQLite dependency in this package, no queries beyond "latest N" needed; `List(limit)` newest-first, corrupt lines skipped, missing file = 0 entries; `Count()`; `LastError()`). Schema: timestamp (UTC RFC3339Nano), tool, caller_kind, caller_id, decision allow/deny, reason, input_hash (SHA-256 hex of canonical JSON args — raw inputs NEVER stored), output_summary (truncated to 200 runes), error_code (0 allow / -32002/-32001/-32602 deny). Every tools/call audited: allow, scope-denied, guardrail-denied, unknown-tool. Production stdio wired in `cmd/codvian-router/doctor_configure_mcp.go` runMCPTo (OpenAuditLog, stderr warning + unaudited run if it cannot be opened — availability over audit completeness; no DataDir = no audit path). No HTTP in this slice; M9.7 reads via `List`/`Count`.
- Proof: `go test ./internal/mcp/ -race -count=1` → 31 top-level PASS / 0 FAIL (was 13 pre-slice; +18 new: 5 scope, 6 audit, 7 enforcement/identity); `go test ./internal/server/ -run 'Mcp|MCP'` PASS; `go build ./...`, `go vet` (mcp + cmd), `gofmt -l` clean. TOGGLE-VERIFY (both restored byte-identical, diff-verified): (1) removing the scope check → TestNarrowKeyDeniedBeforeDispatch, TestAnonymousIdentityDeniedEverything, TestScopeDenialPrecedesGuardrail all FAIL (denied calls dispatched, guardrail code -32001 instead of -32002); (2) disabling the audit write → TestAuditHashPresentRawInputAbsent + TestAuditOutputTruncationAt200 FAIL, TestAuditDeniedCallsLogged panics (index out of range on empty list). Counting-source tests prove denied calls never reach dispatch (Status/Providers/Models call counts stay 0).
- THREAT MODEL (for the security reviewer): STOPS — (a) a narrow/anonymous remote key calling tools outside its scopes (deny before dispatch, -32002, audited); (b) scope confusion with HTTP gateway scopes (disjoint `mcp:` namespace; `chat` is not a known MCP scope); (c) secret leakage through the audit log (inputs hashed only, outputs capped at 200 chars, key material never recorded); (d) destructive tools via a family scope alone (destructive requires family AND `mcp:destructive`); (e) silent widening (scope copies, RegisterToolScopes panics on typos, no off switch to flip). DOES NOT STOP — (a) the local operator itself (by design: stdio = operator trust; a compromised operator process already owns the machine — no MCP-layer control can fix that); (b) a malicious/compromised MCP CLIENT driving allowed read tools at high rate (no rate limiting on stdio tool calls in this slice); (c) audit-log tampering by anyone with data-dir write access (no signing/hash-chain — an attacker who can write the log can rewrite history; the log is evidence, not tamper-proof evidence); (d) audit loss on disk-full/write failure (Record failure is best-effort, call still served, surfaced only via LastError — no alerting wired yet); (e) log growth (no rotation/cap — a busy server grows audit.jsonl unboundedly; M9.7 follow-up); (f) scope enforcement on any transport other than MCP tools/call (HTTP gateway scopes are a separate system — M9.2 must actually build GatewayKeyClient from the key's scopes, not from a default); (g) exfiltration through allowed read outputs (provider/model names and counts are returned by design — they are not secrets, but they do reveal what is configured).
- Honest gaps: (a) no audit rotation/size cap (M9.7 follow-up); (b) audit write failure does not block the call (deliberate, disclosed above); (c) parse errors and non-tools/call methods are not audited (no tool name to record); (d) PARITY.md Area 9 rows NOT flipped here — flips are the M9.10 gate's job (M9.0 already flagged the Scope/Audit/Identity rows stale in its reconciliation entry). CHECKLIST.md row 382 marked Built ✅, Verified blank (independent SECURITY REVIEW pending — A2A stays gated).

- BLOCKING B1 — fail-open default identity (privilege escalation by construction): `New()` defaults to `LocalOperator()` (full `*` grant) and `Handle()` uses that default; the only production caller today is stdio (no live leak — LocalOperator-leak verdict: NO live leak today), but any M9.2 remote transport reusing `New()` + `Handle()` (the natural path) silently grants network callers full access. The only defence is a doc comment. FIX (fix agent): (1) `New()` defaults to `Anonymous()` (deny-all); (2) stdio construction adds explicit `.WithClientIdentity(mcp.LocalOperator())` so stdio behaviour is unchanged; (3) test asserting `New()` defaults to deny-all and stdio explicitly opts into LocalOperator. ## M9.1 SECURITY REVIEW FIX (fix agent, 2026-11-19) — B1 + N1/N2/N3/N7/N9

- B1 (blocking): `New()` now defaults to `Anonymous()` (deny-all, fail-closed) instead of `LocalOperator()` (`internal/mcp/mcp.go`); the stdio construction in `cmd/codvian-router/doctor_configure_mcp.go` runMCPTo adds explicit `.WithClientIdentity(mcp.LocalOperator())`, so stdio behaviour is unchanged — same trust decision, now explicit instead of the silent default. Any future transport reusing `New()` + `Handle()` without opting in gets deny-all, not a full grant. Comments in `mcp.go` (Server field, `New`, `Handle`, `Serve`) updated to state the fail-closed default.
- N1: a tools/call whose params fail to unmarshal is now audited (`mcp.go`): deny entry, error_code -32602, tool name when parseable else "unknown", nil args (hash of "null"). N2: `OpenAuditLog` Chmods the dir 0700 and the file 0600 on open, and `Record` Chmods 0600 best-effort on every append — pre-existing loose 0755/0644 paths are tightened, not just fresh ones. N3: `WithClientIdentity` defensively copies the identity's scopes (new `copyIdentity` helper, same as `GatewayKeyClient`); `HandleWithIdentity` snapshots the identity per call — checked: it stores nothing, so it had no persistent aliasing hole, but the snapshot keeps the in-flight decision stable. N7: (a) new concurrency test (20 goroutines x 25 records = 500, exact count, every line valid JSON); (b) `Record`'s marshal-error path now reads the stored error via locked `LastError()` instead of reading `l.lastErr` unlocked. N9: `RegisterToolScopes` panics on re-registration of an already-registered tool (consistent with its existing programmer-error panics); the original assignment is unchanged by the panicked attempt.
- Tests updated for the new default (old tests assumed fail-open): `newTestServer()` in `mcp_test.go` now opts into `LocalOperator()` explicitly; `TestStdioIdentityIsLocalOperatorFullScope` rewritten as `TestExplicitLocalOperatorFullScope` (explicit opt-in allows all three tools); `TestHandleWithIdentityPerRequest` tail now expects the untouched server default to deny (-32002). New file `internal/mcp/fix_m9_1_test.go`: `TestNewDefaultsToAnonymousDenyAll` (bare `New()` denies all three tools, zero dispatches, audited as anonymous/-32002), `TestMalformedParamsToolsCallIsAudited` (N1, both name-parseable and unparseable cases), `TestAuditOpenTightensLoosePermissions` (N2, seed entry preserved), `TestWithClientIdentityCopiesScopes` (N3), `TestAuditConcurrentRecordNoInterleave` (N7a), `TestRegisterToolScopesPanicsOnReRegistration` (N9, incl. built-in router_status).

No scope-set, audit-schema, or error-code changes; stdio behaviour unchanged.

## Coordinator: MCP tool registry refactor (2026-11-19) — enables Wave 2 parallelism

- Problem: Tools() and callTool() in internal/mcp/mcp.go were a hardcoded 3-tool list + switch. M9.3/M9.4/M9.5 would all have to edit the same function in the same shared worktree — a clobber risk. The scopes side already had the right pattern (global toolScopes map + RegisterToolScopes, designed for M9.3/M9.4 to call from their own files).

Present (Controls != nil, even empty) → NewWithControls, enforced from construction, fail-closed: "controls": {} is valid config and denies every tool. Stated in code comments in both files.
- Duration form: max_duration accepts a Go duration string ("5m", "30s", "1h30m") or a JSON number of nanoseconds (verbatim time.Duration shape); Marshal writes the string form ("5m0s"). Invalid values rejected at load: negative max_calls/max_duration, blank allow/deny entries, malformed/boolean/float durations — all errors naming mcp.controls.*. Unknown fields inside controls ignored, consistent with the rest of config.

Reservation is made before dispatch and is NOT refunded on server error; a failed dispatch counts as one spent call against MaxCalls. Rationale: counting attempts is honest and predictable (successes-only accounting would let a failing tool retry forever against the cap). Documented in both files' comments and pinned by TestNewMCPRegistryFailedDispatchConsumesBudget (failing server, MaxCalls: 1 → CallsUsed == 1 after the failure, next call ErrBudgetExhausted).

A *Server-keyed map alone is invisible to the copy. The Source key fixes it (the copy shares src); lookup prefers the exact server entry, falls back to the Source entry; uncomparable Sources skipped via reflect.Type.Comparable() (no panic; every Source in-tree is comparable). Regression test TestM95ToolFilterSurvivesWithIdentityCopy (two servers, distinct Sources, different filters, both correct through Handle). Edge documented in code: servers sharing one Source share the handler-path filter (last writer wins); cleaner long-term home is a field on Server (coordinator-owned).

- Routes: GET/POST /api/mcp/sse, POST/GET /api/mcp/stream, DELETE /api/mcp/stream/{id}. SSE: GET opens the stream (endpoint event first), POST answers 202 with the response on the stream; closing GET terminates the session. Streamable: POST answers 200 JSON directly (202 for notifications); initialize opens a session (Mcp-Session-Id header); unknown id → 404 (except initialize-with-stale-id → fresh session, auto-recovery); GET stream survives closing; DELETE → 204. GET /api/mcp/status shape untouched (M9.7's handler).
- Toggles: reads M9.7's mcpSSEEnabled/mcpStreamEnabled (single source of truth, no second copy). Disabled → 503; DELETE skips the toggle check so sessions stay terminable when disabled.
- Identity-threading fix (coordinator follow-up): withIdentity(id) returns a shallow Server copy carrying the per-request identity (shares src/guard/audit); handleWithIdentity dispatches via s.withIdentity(id).callTool(...). No signature change, no mutation of the shared server.
- Serve-mode Source (24 methods): live Providers/Models/Status/Combos/RoutingStrategy/PriceKnown/CacheStats/CacheFlush/DBHealth/DBStats/Memory*; honest -32602 not-available errors for CreateCombo, ComboMetrics, SetRoutingStrategy, ExplainRoute, SimulateRoute, ModelStats (empty list), CostReport, CheckQuota, SetBudgetGuard — the M9.3 backend doesn't exist yet in either process. No fake numbers anywhere.
- Decisions (with reasons): GUARD-TABLE SCOPE NONE (scope-free), Tier MANAGEMENT for all 5. No HTTP scope fits: admin would lock out narrow MCP-only keys and the guard treats admin as implying every scope; chat/models would wrongly demand HTTP scopes of an MCP-only key. Precedent: me/status, me/sessions. Auth still required (not exempt); authorisation lives inside the transport in the disjoint mcp: namespace. Consequence: the 5 routes are in the scopeFree allowlist, and TestScopeEnforcedTableDriven got a carve-out (same pattern as its om-usage exception): admin key on the 4 toggle-checked routes → 503 (disabled by default), on DELETE → 403 naming the invalid scope. SCOPE VALIDATION whole-request 403: every scope on the key must be a known mcp: scope or a trailing-* wildcard covering ≥1 known scope (mcp:read:*, mcp:* valid). Bare * rejected; admin/chat/mixed keys rejected naming the scope; empty scope list allowed (every tool call then denied -32002 by the MCP layer). RATE LIMITS (in-memory token buckets): per-key burst 30, refill 1/s (60/min); per-IP burst 120, refill 5/s (300/min). 429 with Retry-After: 1, type mcp_rate_limit. Check order: 401 → 503 → 403 → 429. SESSIONS: crypto/rand 128-bit hex ids; 64 per transport, 16 per key per transport (over cap → 503); 5-min idle eviction (lazy sweep, ≤1/min, never evicts a session with an open stream); one owner key each (foreign key → 404, indistinguishable from unknown); 1 MiB body cap → 413; one JSON-RPC message per POST (no batches, same as stdio). TOGGLE DEFAULT OFF (fail-closed), NOT "enabled" as the brief said — M9.7 landed default OFF and its tests pin it; builder did not flip it. Flagged as a brief-vs-landed conflict — RESOLVED by the M9.7 review: both slices converge on OFF, stated in M9.2's header comment (mcp_http.go:28-35). Restart restores OFF, not enabled. PER-SERVER STATE: package-level sync.Map keyed by *Server in mcp_http.go, so server.go stays route-wiring-only.

- Toggle (scratch copy only, sha256-verified byte-identical restores; scratch ~/workspace/m92-review-scratch deleted afterwards; real tree never modified, git status unchanged at the end, 36 pre-existing Wave 2 entries, zero reviewer artifacts): Toggle A (validMCPGrant → always true): TestMCPHTTPStarScopeRejected, TestMCPHTTPAdminAndChatScopesRejected, TestValidMCPGrant all FAIL; the star key gets HTTP 200 with a full initialize result — i.e. the filter is the only thing standing between a * key and the full grant. Restored (sha256 b9f98a73…). Toggle B (allowKey → always true): TestMCPHTTPRateLimit FAILS with 0× 429s. Restored. Toggle C (s.withIdentity(id).callTool → s.callTool in mcp.go:613): HTTP identity test FAILS (tenant "anonymous", want gateway-key id gk_c62343eb6ff63917); concurrent test FAILS (all 16 goroutines see tenant "anonymous", 0 flushes each). Bug A reintroduced (bare-* rejection removed from validMCPGrant): validMCPGrant("*") = true, star key gets 200. The trap is real and the explicit rejection is load-bearing. Restored. Bug B reintroduced (sess, err := shadowing in the stale-id branch): nil-pointer panic in TestMCPHTTPUnknownSession. The assignment fix is load-bearing. Restored.
- N4 PRECONDITION — SATISFIED: mcpIdentityForRequest (mcp_http.go:536-558) builds GatewayKeyClient(keyID, scopes) from the key's validated scopes only; every scope must pass validMCPGrant or the request is 403 naming the scope. validMCPGrant (:460-482) rejects bare * explicitly first with a comment explaining the empty-prefix trap; mcp:* / mcp:read:* pass; admin/chat/models/mixed keys rejected naming the scope (tests TestMCPHTTPStarScopeRejected, TestMCPHTTPAdminAndChatScopesRejected, TestValidMCPGrant — 21 table cases incl. mcp:evil:*, mcp:*:status, mcp:). The HTTP guard treats admin as implying every scope (server.go:1644-1646, key.HasScope(want) || key.HasScope("admin")) but the transport never calls requireScope (guard rows carry no scopes) and never passes HTTP scopes through — confirmed by code + the admin-key tests. Empty scope list allowed; every tool call then denied -32002 (TestMCPHTTPEmptyScopeKey). No path exists where an HTTP scope becomes an MCP grant.
- IDENTITY THREADING — SATISFIED: zero LocalOperator() constructions on any network path: repo-wide grep finds exactly one production construction, stdio cmd/codvian-router/doctor_configure_mcp.go:1682 (explicit opt-in). The transport's only dispatch sites are HandleWithIdentity at mcp_http.go:780 (SSE POST) and :871 (Streamable POST); the only non-test .Handle( call in the repo is stdio Serve (mcp.go:659). withIdentity (mcp.go:369-385) is a shallow copy sharing src/guard/audit, copying scopes, never mutating the shared server; concurrent test (16 goroutines) re-run green, server identity stays Anonymous. mcp.Server has no mutex fields, so the copy is vet-clean.
- RATE LIMITING — SATISFIED, exactly as claimed: per-key burst 30 / refill 1/s, per-IP burst 120 / refill 5/s (mcp_http.go:148-157); 429 with Retry-After: 1, type mcp_rate_limit (:571-582). Reviewer's probe measured the exact counts the shipped test only bounds loosely: 30×404 then 15×429 over 45 requests, stable across 6 runs; other key unaffected. White-box probe of the per-IP bucket (no shipped test covers it): exactly 120/130 allowed, different IP unaffected. Check order in code is 401 → 503 → 403 → 429 (mcpTransportGate, :594-618). HONEST LIMITATION: limiter is per-process in-memory — a restart resets it. Acceptable for this slice (documented in the header comment; no persistence was in the brief), but it means a restart also resets an attacker's spent budget.
- SESSIONS — SATISFIED: newSessionID uses crypto/rand 16 bytes → 32 lowercase hex (:340-347); caps 64/transport, 16/key → 503 (:357-372, white-box test + handler 503 mapping); 5-min idle eviction, lazy sweep ≤1/min, never evicts streams > 0 (:307-324); one owner key, foreign key → 404 indistinguishable from unknown (TestMCPHTTPSessionIsolation, incl. foreign DELETE → 404 and post-DELETE 404); 1 MiB cap → 413 (probed); one message per POST, batch → -32700 parse error, never dispatched (probed). SSE close terminates the session (POST after close → 404, TestMCPHTTPSSEFlow); Streamable GET close does NOT terminate (session answers POSTs after close, then DELETE → 204 → 404, TestMCPHTTPStreamableGETStream) — and it is not a leak: the deferred streams-- + touch re-arms idle eviction, and DELETE remains available.
- TOGGLES/DEFAULT — SATISFIED, fail-closed: disabled → 503 on the 4 toggle-checked routes; DELETE skips the toggle (checkToggle=false, :594) so sessions stay terminable when disabled. Default is OFF in code (zero-value bools, server.go:647-648) and pinned by TestHandleMCPStatusShape (exact body {"sse":{"enabled":false,"sessions":0},"streamable":{"enabled":false,"sessions":0}}) and TestMCPHTTPTransportToggle. The brief said "default enabled" but both slices landed OFF; the landed behavior is stated honestly in the header comment (mcp_http.go:28-35). Reviewer does not re-litigate the brief; the landed behavior is fail-closed and consistent.
- GUARD-TABLE SCOPE-FREE DECISION — JUDGED CORRECT: all 5 routes Tier MANAGEMENT, no scopes, not AuthExempt (guard.go:133-137, comment :126-132). Auth is still required twice over: the guard wraps Tier MANAGEMENT non-exempt routes in requireAuthUnlessRelaxed (guard.go:503-512), and the handlers unconditionally refuse keyless requests via mcpRequireKey (mcp_http.go:519-527) — reviewer probed keyless → 401 on all 5 routes EVEN in auth-relaxed mode (WithAuthRelaxed(true)), which is the case where the guard alone would pass keyless through. Authorisation lives entirely inside the transport in the disjoint mcp: namespace. The scopeFree allowlist entry (guard_test.go:839-843) and the TestScopeEnforcedTableDriven carve-out (:707-730, same pattern as the om-usage exception) are honest: admin key on the 4 toggle-checked routes → 503 (disabled by default) and on DELETE → 403 naming the invalid scope. No guard invariant is weakened; no bypass.
- SERVE-MODE SOURCE (24 methods) — HONEST: all 8 not-available methods probed over live HTTP with a properly scoped manage key: create_combo, get_combo_metrics, set_routing_strategy, explain_route, simulate_route, cost_report, check_quota, set_budget_guard all answer -32602 with messages naming the missing backend ("unavailable: mcp: … is not available over the MCP HTTP transport yet — …"). ModelStats returns [] (mcp_http.go:1219-1225); no fake numbers anywhere in the adapter (grep-verified; live Providers/Models/Status/Combos/CacheStats/DBHealth/DBStats read real server state). See N4 for the one wrinkle in how the empty ModelStats surfaces.
- AUDIT — SATISFIED: the transport's mcpServer() wires the audit log (mcp_http.go:433-446: s.mcpAudit if set, else the data-dir log — so HTTP calls are audited even though serve mode hasn't wired WithMCPAuditLog yet). Reviewer's probe: after a remote cache_flush, the audit file contains caller_kind: "gateway-key", caller_id: "gk_69aa0c6a521495bf" (the key id, not "anonymous"), decision: "allow", and a SHA-256 input_hash (no raw args). Attribution works.
- SECRETS / GROWTH — CLEAN: no key material in logs or responses: only two Printf lines in the transport (mcp_http.go:444, :790), carrying a session id + key id and an audit-open error; bearerKey is used only to derive the key id (SHA-256) and to ask the verifier for scopes (:502, :542). Session caps + eviction verified; outbox bounded at 128 with oldest-drop + log (:158, :782-791); rate-limiter buckets sweep idle entries past 4096 (see N7).
- KNOWN GAPS — confirmed as stated, not worse: Status sessions stays 0 (N1, coordinator-owned): confirmed by probe — live registry counts() returned streamable=1 while GET /api/mcp/status reported {"sessions":0} for both transports. mcpTransportStatus hardcodes 0 (internal/server/mcp.go:235-236); counts() (mcp_http.go:404-410) has NO non-test callers. The endpoint is the only liar; no security decision consumes the count. The now-false comments (mcp.go:47-50, server.go:639-646 — "no transport server in this tree yet") should be fixed with the seam. 3 tool-count pins (N2, coordinator-owned): TestHandleMCP (mcp_test.go:52), TestHandleMCPEmptyState (:91), TestHandleMCPSelectorFeed (mcploop_test.go:239) assert 3 tools; the registry holds 24. All three are count pins in M9.7's test files, not M9.2 logic — the 21 extra tools are sibling registrations (M9.3 12, M9.4 7, M9.5 2), zero from this slice.
- NITS (all coordinator-owned, none blocking): N3 (MOST IMPORTANT follow-up — latent, merge-wave): the M9.3 cost handlers take key_id from caller ARGS (tools_cost.go:173-230 — reqStringArg(args, "key_id")), not from the identity (contrast the ops tools, which use s.callerID()). Today this is harmless: all three methods answer honest -32602 before the arg is used (probed with an arbitrary foreign key id gk_someotherkey — no data accessed). But the moment the merge wave wires serverMCPSource.CostReport/CheckQuota/SetBudgetGuard to the ledger, a remote mcp:manage:cost key could read/set ANY key's quota/budget. THE WIRING MUST FORCE key_id = caller identity (or have the adapter override the arg). Flagging now so it is not wired naively. N4: pick_fastest_model over HTTP errors "no measured model latency yet" when ledger data exists but is unwired (ModelStats returns []) — it errors rather than fabricating a fastest model, so it is not a trust defect — but the message is literally inaccurate over HTTP; it should say the backend is unavailable. N5: a batch containing an initialize member creates an orphan session (probed: stream sessions 0→1, Mcp-Session-Id returned) even though the batch is rejected with -32700. Bounded by the caps + idle eviction; consider not creating the session until the message parses as a single object. N6: the shipped rate-limit test asserts loose bounds (≥5 429s, ≥25 404s) though exact counts are stable (30/15, 6 runs); and no shipped test exercises the per-IP bucket (probed white-box: exact). Tighten the test; add an IP-bucket case. N7: rate-limiter bucket map has no hard cap (eviction only sweeps idle->10min entries once len > 4096); mcpHTTPStates sync.Map never deletes per-*Server entries. Production has one server; the strong key references also prevent pointer-reuse staleness, so this is robustness-only. N8 (inherited, M9.5 F1): wire-path cardinality (WithToolFilter) is still unenforced in handleWithIdentity — but M9.2 never configures a filter on the transport server, so there is nothing to bypass today. Coordinator-owned, as the M9.5 review ruled. N9: SSE POST looks the session up under the lock but uses it after unlock; a concurrent terminate can only orphan a response into the (bounded, drop-on-full) outbox. Benign race window. N10: an empty-scope key can open sessions and hold up to 16 slots doing nothing — within caps, evicted after 5 min idle. Acceptable.
- A2A (M9.8) STAYS UNBLOCKED: it was unblocked by the M9.1 re-review PASS; this review finds no blocker for M9.2 and nothing that re-blocks M9.8. The one item that could become a privilege issue later (N3, cost-tool key_id from args) is not live today and is a merge-wave wiring condition, not an M9.2 or M9.8 blocker.
